## 2026-08-23 — Multi-Tenant Program: Phase B3 implementation plan approved, Phase 0 (Platform Foundation) implemented locally

A separate, longer-running initiative from the Election Day UX/security work documented elsewhere in this file: a staged plan to migrate KolBox's currently single-tenant Election Day system (one Supabase project = one campaign, no multi-tenancy - CLAUDE.md's "Non-negotiable requirements") to a Multi-Tenant architecture (Platform → Election Workspace, with Platform Owner / Election Owner / Multi-Entity Owner as new principals alongside the existing Manager/caller roles). This section covers planning plus Phase 0's schema foundation only - no existing Election Day table, RLS policy, RPC, or frontend behavior has changed, and no second real workspace exists anywhere.

**Approved target architecture (summary - the full plan was produced as in-conversation reports this session, not yet a standalone document)**: one shared Supabase/Postgres database, every Election-Day-scoped record gets `workspace_id`, deny-by-default RLS enforced server-side (never only client-side), stable-ID relationships only. Platform Owner (Supabase Auth + mandatory MFA, server-side-only operations, cannot touch workspace operational data) and Multi-Entity Owner (exactly one at a time, Supabase Auth, read-only aggregate view of assigned ACTIVE workspaces) are new platform-level principals. Election Owner is a new, separate system principal per workspace (not a dynamic role, Supabase Auth, multiple equal Owners allowed, last remaining Owner can never be deleted). Managers/callers/ordinary users keep today's username+password model but move to a real server-side session (HttpOnly+Secure+SameSite cookie, hashed token, CSRF-protected, no authoritative role/workspace/token in localStorage) - **ordinary usernames stay globally unique across all of KolBox** (already true today: `election_day_permission_users.name` already carries a global UNIQUE constraint), so login never requires the user to identify a workspace/slug/code: the server resolves `username → PermissionUser row → workspace_id` entirely server-side, and the browser is never a source of truth for workspace*id. Passwords move to Argon2id (or a justified equivalent) computed in a Vercel Server Function, since Postgres's `pgcrypto` extension has no Argon2id support - today's in-DB `election_day_login` RPC's bcrypt-in-Postgres pattern cannot be reused as-is. Ordinary Managers/callers have **zero direct browser/PostgREST access** - every read and every write for that user class goes through trusted Server/RPC paths; RLS-backed direct reads apply only where a real Supabase Auth principal (Platform/Election/Multi-Entity Owner) can be securely scoped via `auth.uid()`. Workspace creation is deliberately two-step: Platform Owner creates only \_pending* Owner access (name/phone/email + a temporary password set directly in Supabase Auth, **never stored in our own tables**); the actual `election_workspaces` row is created only later, atomically, when that Owner's first login + workspace-name/END-timestamp confirmation completes. Hard Delete only (no soft delete, no restore) - a DB transaction removes all workspace data + writes a minimal permanent `platform_deletion_audit` row, followed by an idempotent/retry-safe Vercel-orchestrated Supabase Admin API cleanup of the workspace's Owner Auth accounts.

**Final approved post-END session rule** (the plan's one open product decision, now resolved): if a Manager/caller logs in _before_ the workspace's configured `election_end_at`, their session expires at `election_end_at` exactly as already documented above. Reaching `election_end_at` does **not** lock the system - authorized users may keep working and may log in again after END. If a user logs in **after** `election_end_at` has already passed, that new session instead expires **8 hours after that login** (since "expire at END" is meaningless for a login that starts after END has already passed). No automatic Election Day lock at END, ever, under any circumstance.

**Planning process**: a read-only Phase A architecture audit and a Phase B1 entity-model validation preceded this session's Phase B3 work (both delivered as in-conversation reports in an earlier session, not persisted to this file). This session's Phase B3 pass re-verified the live repository directly (4 parallel read-only research agents covering schema/RLS/migrations, auth/session/permissions, RPC/business-logic, and frontend/routes/storage) and produced a complete CURRENT→TARGET mapping, full migration dependency graph, staged Expand→Backfill→Cutover→Contract phase plan (Phase 0 Platform Foundation → Phase 1 additive `workspace_id` columns → Phase 2 Backfill the existing Production dataset into one workspace → Phase 3 NOT NULL/constraint tightening → Phase 4 RLS deny-by-default rebuild → Phase 5 RPC workspace-scoping → Phase 6 auth/session transition → Phase 7 frontend cutover → Contract), a security verification matrix, and a risk/contradiction list - then revised twice against explicit corrections (removing an unnecessary workspace-slug/code login step, since usernames are already globally unique; removing all Owner password/credential material from the app's own `election_workspace_pending_owner_access` table, since Supabase Auth is the sole credential store).

**Known open items, carried forward into Phase 1+ (none block Phase 0, none decided unilaterally)**: whether the _main_ campaign-management app (voter classification/activists/dashboard - still `MockApi`/localStorage, never mentioned anywhere in the approved architecture) is in scope for this Multi-Tenant program at all; no email exists today for any `election_day_permission_users` row, so the first real Election Owner(s) for Production's eventual backfilled workspace must be a deliberate human input in Phase 2, not derivable from existing data; no historical `election_end_at` exists for Production's current dataset, so Phase 2's backfill needs an explicit administrative value or a real one collected up front; the main app's `profiles` table has no tracked migration anywhere in this repo, relevant only if the main-app-scope question above resolves toward "in scope."

**Phase 0 (Platform Foundation) - implemented and verified locally.** Strictly additive: 7 brand-new tables (`platform_owners`, `election_workspaces`, `election_workspace_pending_owner_access`, `election_owners`, `multi_entity_owner`, `multi_entity_assignments`, `platform_deletion_audit`), zero touch to any existing `election_day_*` table/RLS/RPC, zero real workspace or Owner row created. `election_workspace_pending_owner_access` deliberately carries no password/password_hash column of any kind - only `auth_user_id → auth.users(id)`, matching the corrected design. `election_workspaces` deliberately carries no stored status/is_active/deleted_at column - "ACTIVE" stays purely derived from `election_end_at`, never a second stored source of truth. `multi_entity_owner` is a true singleton via the same `id boolean primary key default true` idiom this project already uses for `election_day_settings` (here the cardinality genuinely matches the product rule, unlike `election_day_settings`' own case). Every one of the 7 tables is RLS-enabled with zero policies and zero grants to `anon`/`authenticated` - deny-by-default, matching this project's own established pattern for every other sensitive table. New migration: `supabase/migrations/20260823010000_multi_tenant_phase0_platform_foundation.sql`. New regression: `scripts/smoke-multi-tenant-phase0-schema.ts` (42/42 checks, pure text-parse of the migration file, no DB connection required, same style as `smoke-role-seed-parity.ts`).

**Verification (local Docker + one disposable isolated project only - nothing pushed, nothing applied to Production)**: live schema introspection confirmed all 7 tables RLS-enabled with 0 policies and 0 `SELECT`/`INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` grants to `anon`/`authenticated` (the only grants present - `TRUNCATE`/`REFERENCES`/`TRIGGER` - were confirmed to be Supabase's own pre-existing platform-wide default privilege, identical on the already-Production-live `election_day_permission_users` table, not something this migration introduces); all 22 constraints (PKs/FKs/UNIQUEs/CHECKs) match the migration file exactly. A disposable functional test (4 fake `auth.users` rows, fully cleaned up after) proved: a second `multi_entity_owner` row is correctly rejected with `unique_violation` (the singleton is real, not just documented), and deleting an `election_workspaces` row correctly cascade-deletes its `election_owners`/`multi_entity_assignments` rows - zero residual rows anywhere afterward, including the disposable `auth.users` accounts. Existing local business-data baseline reconfirmed untouched (1 voter, 3 permission users, 3 roles). An isolated disposable fresh-replay project (`kolboxphase0replay`, unique ports, zero shared containers/volumes with the main stack) proved all 50 migrations (49 existing + this new one) replay cleanly from empty with zero errors, then was fully torn down. `tsc -b`/`npm run build` clean (output bundle hashes identical to the already-verified Production build, confirming zero frontend impact, as expected since Phase 0 touched no `src/` file). A representative sample of existing smoke tests re-run unchanged (`smoke-role-seed-parity`, `smoke-election-day-scope`, `smoke-role-catalog`, `smoke-role-management-logic`, `smoke-bootstrap` all pass; `smoke-permissions` fails on the same known, pre-existing, unrelated stale-assertion gap already documented elsewhere in this project - not touched, not a regression). All 15 protected `scripts/drive-*`/`scripts/smoke-*` files confirmed untouched throughout (Phase 0 added exactly 2 new files and touched nothing else).

**Status: local implementation approved and committed locally - not pushed, not deployed, no migration applied to Production, Phase 1 not started.** See `task-plan.md`'s Progress Log and `git log` for the exact commit.

## 2026-08-26 — Multi-Tenant Program Phase 3A: local Vercel `/api` routing canary verified (local-only, not deployed)

Phase 3A's trusted-workspace-context architecture (browser → HttpOnly cookie → Vercel Server Function → session-hash-based RPC; full design worked out across several in-conversation design/correction passes this session, not yet persisted as its own document) requires confirming that a Vercel Serverless Function under `/api/*` actually takes precedence over `vercel.json`'s existing SPA catch-all rewrite (`/(.*)  → /index.html`) before any real session/auth code is built on that assumption.

**Implemented**: `api/health.ts` - a minimal, dependency-free canary handler (`{ ok: true }`, HTTP 200), no DB access, no secrets, no external calls. No other file changed; no new dependency added (`@vercel/node` was not needed - local `tsc -b` and `eslint` both pass against the plain-JS-shaped handler signature).

**Verified locally, via `vercel dev --listen 3111` only (no `vercel build`/deploy, no Production touched)**: `GET /api/health` → `200`, `content-type: application/json`, body `{"ok":true}` - served by the function, not the rewrite. `GET /voters` (an ordinary SPA route) → `200`, `content-type: text/html`, the app's own `index.html` - confirming the SPA catch-all still works unchanged for everything outside `/api/*`. This empirically confirms (not just documented-behavior inference, per the prior design turn's open item) that no `vercel.json` change is needed for `/api/*` functions to route correctly on this project. The local dev server process was fully stopped after verification; nothing was deployed, pushed, or applied to Production.

**Checks**: `npx tsc -b` clean, `npx eslint api/health.ts` clean (after one `prettier --write` formatting fix), `git diff --check` clean. `git status` shows only `api/health.ts` (new, untracked) plus the same 15 pre-existing protected `scripts/drive-*`/`scripts/smoke-*` files (untouched by this work, as always).

**Status: local-only proof-of-routing complete. Not committed, not pushed, not deployed. Session/auth implementation itself has not started** - this canary only proves the routing precondition the design relies on.

## 2026-08-26 — Multi-Tenant Program Phase 3A: first EXPAND migration authored locally (written only, not applied anywhere)

`supabase/migrations/20260826000000_multi_tenant_phase3a_expand_sessions_foundation.sql` - the durable schema foundation for trusted PermissionUser sessions: `UNIQUE (workspace_id, id)` on `election_day_permission_users` (deliberately without `NOT NULL` - deferred to a later ENFORCE phase, since the still-live legacy/`_v2` create-user paths omit `workspace_id`); new `election_day_sessions` table (composite FK to `election_day_permission_users(workspace_id, id)`, `ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE RESTRICT`, hash-only `token_hash`, no raw-token column, no `revoked_at`, no sliding expiry); new `election_day_login_attempts` table (schema-only foundation for the approved Postgres-backed rate-limit design, no RPC yet); a new nullable `action` column plus a new composite FK on `election_day_reauth_proofs` (its `workspace_id` column already existed from Phase 1 - only the composite FK is new). Both new tables are RLS-enabled with zero policies, matching this project's own established pattern for every other RPC-only table.

**This migration has NOT been applied anywhere - not to local Docker, not to Production.** Only static review was performed this pass: exact-SQL inspection, dependency/statement ordering, a per-callsite legacy-compatibility proof against the current, live bodies of `election_day_create_permission_user`/`_v2`, `election_day_delete_permission_user`/`_v2`, `election_day_login`, and `election_day_reauth`/`election_day_verify_reauth_proof`/`election_day_revoke_reauth_proof` (none of their INSERT/DELETE/SELECT statements are affected), and an ACL/RLS precedent review. **Runtime/local-DB migration execution (does it actually apply cleanly, do the constraints behave as designed against real data) remains unverified and requires a separate, explicit next approval before `supabase db push` (local, then only later `--linked`) runs.**

## 2026-08-26 — Multi-Tenant Program Phase 3A: EXPAND migration ACL-hardened and locally applied + runtime-verified (still NOT applied to Production)

The EXPAND migration above (`20260826000000_multi_tenant_phase3a_expand_sessions_foundation.sql`) was hardened with explicit `REVOKE ALL ... FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated` on both new tables (`election_day_sessions`, `election_day_login_attempts`), on top of their existing RLS-enabled/zero-policy baseline - defense-in-depth mirroring this project's own Permanent Engineering Guardrail (name every role explicitly, never rely on a bare `REVOKE ... FROM PUBLIC` alone), applied here to table privileges rather than the guardrail's original function-EXECUTE context. No sequence privileges were relevant (neither table uses a serial/IDENTITY column).

**Applied to a fresh, disposable LOCAL Docker Supabase stack only** (`supabase start --ignore-health-check` - the local `storage-api` container's own health check failed for reasons unrelated to Postgres/the migration chain; DB and every other service came up healthy). All 55 migrations in the repo, including this new one, applied cleanly end-to-end from empty with zero errors. **Never touched the linked Production project** - confirmed via `supabase status` before any DB command (local stack was not even running yet; the linked ref shown was informational only, never targeted).

## 2026-08-26 — Multi-Tenant Program Phase 3A: Session/Reauth v3 foundation implemented and runtime-verified locally (NOT applied to Production, NOT committed)

Implements the Session + action-bound Reauth v3 RPCs and the `/api/election-day/session` Vercel endpoint described by the trusted-workspace-context architecture (browser → `__Host-kb_ed_session` HttpOnly cookie → Vercel Server Function → hash credential in Node → service_role-only RPC → DB derives actor/workspace/role). Strictly additive - no existing table, RLS policy, or legacy/`_v2` function touched; `election_day_login`/`election_day_reauth`/every `_v2` admin RPC remain unchanged, unretired, and still anon/authenticated-reachable exactly as before. No frontend login cutover of any kind.

**New migration**: `supabase/migrations/20260826010000_multi_tenant_phase3a_session_rpcs.sql` (companion to the already-Production-applied `20260826000000` EXPAND migration) - 7 new functions:

- `election_day_resolve_session(p_session_hash bytea)` - service_role-only. Resolves `{actor_id, actor_name, role_id, workspace_id}` from a session hash, `expires_at > now()` required, generic `UNAUTHORIZED` on every failure mode.
- `election_day_register_login_attempt(p_bucket_key text)` - service_role-only, standalone. Atomic fixed-15-minute-window upsert counter, self-resetting, opportunistic 1-hour cleanup.
- `election_day_login_v2(p_name, p_password, p_session_hash)` - service_role-only. bcrypt-verifies credentials, requires non-null `workspace_id` (a NULL-workspace legacy account cannot log in this path - generic `UNAUTHORIZED`, matching the EXPAND migration's own documented risk), creates the session row in the same transaction as successful auth, fixed 24h expiry.
- `election_day_logout_v2(p_session_hash bytea)` - service_role-only, idempotent physical delete by hash.
- `election_day_reauth_v3(p_session_hash, p_password, p_action, p_proof_hash)` - service_role-only. Derives actor/workspace from the session (never a client-supplied id), still requires the actor's own password (step-up), issues a proof bound to exactly one `p_action`, 5-minute expiry.
- `election_day_verify_reauth_proof_v3(p_session_hash, p_proof_hash, p_action)` - internal-only (zero grants, not even service_role). Requires a currently-valid session AND a matching non-expired same-actor/same-workspace/same-action proof.
- `election_day_create_permission_user_v3(p_session_hash, p_reauth_proof_hash, p_name, p_password, p_role_id)` - service_role-only. Closes the EXPAND migration's own "legacy-create-user NULL-workspace risk": writes the ACTING CALLER'S OWN `workspace_id` (resolved server-side) onto the new row, unlike `_v2` which never writes `workspace_id` at all. `election_day_create_permission_user_v2` is untouched and remains the frontend's only creation path - this RPC exists ahead of a separate, later, not-yet-authorized frontend cutover decision.

ACL: every function explicitly `REVOKE`s from `PUBLIC`/`anon`/`authenticated` by name (per CLAUDE.md's Permanent Engineering Guardrail); the 6 directly-callable functions above additionally `GRANT EXECUTE` to `service_role` only - the browser's anon key can never call any of them, unlike every legacy/`_v2` roster RPC. `election_day_verify_reauth_proof_v3` has zero grants at all (internal-helper-only, matching `election_day_verify_reauth_proof`'s existing precedent).

**Two real bugs found by local runtime testing and fixed before this design was ever applied anywhere - not caught by static review**:

1. **Ambiguous-column bug** (a known bug class in this codebase - see `20260806210000`'s prior `id`-ambiguity fix): `election_day_login_v2` and `election_day_reauth_v3` both declare `RETURNS TABLE` with an OUT parameter named `expires_at` (and `actor_id`/`action` for reauth_v3) that shadowed the identically-named table column inside their own cleanup `DELETE` statements, raising a genuine `42702` error. Fixed by aliasing every such table reference and qualifying every column through the alias - now the standing discipline for every function in this migration that returns a table.
2. **Rate-limiter architecture flaw**: the original design registered the login attempt *inside* `election_day_login_v2`, before credential verification. Local testing (14 consecutive wrong-password attempts) proved this never actually throttled - Postgres rolls back every write made earlier in a function invocation when that invocation later raises an exception (the `UNAUTHORIZED` on the wrong password), undoing the attempt-count increment along with it. Fixed by making `election_day_register_login_attempt` a standalone, directly service_role-callable RPC that the Vercel endpoint invokes as its own separate call/transaction *before* ever calling `election_day_login_v2` - the count now commits independently of whatever the subsequent login call does.

**Local DB runtime verification** (disposable local Docker Supabase only, `supabase db reset --local` replayed all 57 migrations cleanly from empty including this one, all fixtures tagged/cleaned up, 0 residual rows confirmed after every pass): 24/24 scenario checks passed - login success creates exactly one session; bad password / unknown username / NULL-workspace user all fail with the same generic `UNAUTHORIZED`; correct hash resolves the correct actor/workspace/role; invalid hash and a manually-inserted expired session are both rejected; logout invalidates the session and is idempotent on a repeat/null call; deleting a PermissionUser cascades their session; the rate limiter throttles starting at exactly the 11th attempt in a bucket (threshold 10/15-min-window) and a distinct bucket starts fresh; reauth_v3 issues a proof only on correct password; verify_reauth_proof_v3 accepts the correct action, rejects a different action on the same proof, rejects a cross-actor proof, rejects an expired proof; the stored `token_hash`/`proof_hash` never literal-matches the raw text passed in (only the sha256 digest matches); `create_permission_user_v3` correctly inherits the acting caller's own `workspace_id` and is `FORBIDDEN` for a role without `electionDay.manageUsers` even with a fully valid session+proof; every legacy RPC (`election_day_login`, `election_day_reauth`, `election_day_create_permission_user_v2`, `election_day_delete_permission_user_v2`) still functions unchanged; direct `anon`/`authenticated` role calls to all 7 new functions are denied (`insufficient_privilege`) - 7/7. A separate genuine-concurrency test (two truly parallel `docker exec psql` processes, 20 increments each against the same bucket key) confirmed the atomic upsert loses zero updates (40/40 counted).

**Local API runtime verification** (`vercel dev --listen 3111`, local Supabase Docker stack, local-only service_role credential from `supabase status`): `/api/health` and SPA routing (`/voters`) both still work unaffected. Full `/api/election-day/session` flow proven end-to-end against a disposable test account: `POST` with correct credentials → `200`, sets `__Host-kb_ed_session` cookie with exactly `HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict; Path=/; Max-Age=86400`, raw token never appears in the JSON body; `GET` with that cookie → `200`, resolves the correct identity; `DELETE` → `200`, clears the cookie; `GET` after `DELETE` → `401`. Origin validation confirmed on `POST`/`DELETE` (a non-allowlisted `Origin` → `403 FORBIDDEN_ORIGIN`; `GET` deliberately has no Origin check, since browsers do not reliably send one on a same-origin simple GET). Rate limiting confirmed live through the real endpoint (11 consecutive wrong-password calls → `429 RATE_LIMITED` from roughly the 8th call onward in this run, since the IP-bucket had already accumulated some count from earlier requests in the same test session - correct behavior, not a bug, since either bucket exceeding the threshold throttles by design). No raw Postgres exception/stack ever reached a response body in any test - always one of the fixed generic codes.

**A credential-handling incident occurred and was corrected during this pass, disclosed here per CLAUDE.md's own precedent for such incidents**: `npx supabase start`'s own stdout (which cannot be suppressed) printed the local Docker stack's demo `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`/`ANON_KEY`/`JWT_SECRET`/S3 keys directly into a tool-output stream. This is Supabase CLI's well-known, hardcoded local-dev-only default (the literal, publicly-documented `JWT_SECRET="super-secret-jwt-token-with-at-least-32-characters-long"`, unmodified/not customized for this project), valid only against `127.0.0.1:54321` on the machine it ran on, guarding a disposable container that has since been torn down - not a Production credential, and no invalidation action was needed or taken (unlike the earlier Vercel CLI PAT incident). Every subsequent local credential extraction this pass was routed through non-echoing shell redirection into gitignored `.env*`-pattern files, verified via `git check-ignore` before use, and all such temporary files were deleted at the end of this pass (`.env` and `.env.local` were both temporarily modified for the `vercel dev` runtime test and restored byte-for-byte from a backup afterward, diff-confirmed clean).

**Quality**: `npx tsc -b` clean; `npx eslint` clean (0 errors) on both new files; `npx prettier --write` applied; `git diff --check` clean on both new files. A representative sample of pre-existing local smoke tests (`smoke-role-catalog`) re-run unchanged, still passing - confirming no regression outside the touched area. All 15 protected `scripts/drive-*`/`scripts/smoke-*` files and `api/health.ts` re-confirmed SHA-256-identical to their pre-existing baseline throughout this entire pass.

**Files**: `supabase/migrations/20260826010000_multi_tenant_phase3a_session_rpcs.sql` (new), `api/election-day/session.ts` (new). Both currently **untracked/uncommitted**.

**Explicitly NOT done, per this task's own scope**: no migration applied to local-persisted-forward state beyond this verification pass's own `db reset` (the local Docker stack itself has since been stopped - `supabase stop` - so nothing here persists locally either); no Production DB/Auth/Vercel env mutation of any kind; no `git add`/commit/push/deploy; no frontend login cutover (the existing `ElectionDaySession`/`electionDayLoginScreen` flow is completely untouched and still calls only the legacy `election_day_login`); no business-mutation RPC cutover (`import_voters_v3`/`set_voted_v3`/reminder-and-call families/settings/RLS retirement/`_v2` retirement/Phase 3b `NOT NULL` enforcement) - this milestone is Session/Auth foundation only, exactly as scoped.

**Status: Session/Reauth v3 foundation is implemented and runtime-verified end-to-end locally (DB + real HTTP API), including two real bugs found and fixed by that verification. NOT applied to Production. NOT committed. Frontend login cutover has NOT occurred.** Next step: a separate, explicit decision on (a) committing this work, (b) applying the new migration to Production, and (c) beginning the actual frontend cutover to the new session-based login - none of that is authorized by this pass alone.

### Follow-up (2026-08-26) - rate-limit threshold correction: separate username/IP limits

A read-only discrepancy/security review of the above (same day) resolved a migration-count reporting error (the prior "57 migrations" line was a prose mistake - the repo has 56, matching the expected 55 + this one new file; Migration 55 confirmed byte-identical/untouched throughout), verified the IP-trust design against Vercel's own current documentation (`x-forwarded-for` is edge-overwritten by Vercel and does not forward external/client-supplied IPs on this project's direct-to-Vercel topology - not spoofable as implemented), and otherwise passed the rate-limit/session/reauth contract review in full.

That review also flagged the single shared 10-attempts/15-minute threshold (applied identically to both the username and IP buckets) as too restrictive for a real election-day office network, where many real staff can share one NAT'd IP. **Corrected**: `api/election-day/session.ts` now enforces two independent constants - `MAX_USERNAME_ATTEMPTS_PER_WINDOW = 10` (unchanged, still the meaningful per-account brute-force protection) and `MAX_IP_ATTEMPTS_PER_WINDOW = 100` (raised, a coarser-grained shared-network allowance). No SQL change was needed or made - `election_day_register_login_attempt`/`election_day_login_v2` only ever counted and returned attempts; threshold enforcement has always lived in the endpoint, not the RPC, so Migration 55 and the session-rpcs migration (`20260826010000`) are both untouched by this correction.

**Verified locally** (fresh local Docker stack, `supabase start` -> 56/56 migrations confirmed applied, all disposable test data cleaned up after, local stack stopped again afterward): username bucket - attempts 1-10 via the real HTTP endpoint all reached authentication (`401` on a wrong password, proving `election_day_login_v2` was actually called each time), attempt 11 correctly `429 RATE_LIMITED` before `login_v2` was ever invoked; IP bucket - seeded to 95 via 95 direct, already-proven-atomic `election_day_register_login_attempt` calls, then 6 further live HTTP requests (each a distinct, never-reused username, so no individual username threshold could confound the result) landed on counts 96-101: 96-100 all correctly reached authentication, 101 correctly `429 RATE_LIMITED` - proving both the exact new boundary and that IP throttling correctly aggregates across different usernames sharing one IP. Username throttling was independently reconfirmed to still trigger while the IP bucket stayed far below its own threshold (11 vs 100) in the same test run. Concurrency re-confirmed on the unchanged atomic upsert (two genuinely parallel processes, 15+15 increments, 30/30 counted, zero lost updates). ACL re-confirmed unchanged: both `election_day_register_login_attempt` and `election_day_login_v2` remain `service_role`-only (`postgres=X/postgres,service_role=X/postgres`, no `anon`/`authenticated`). A repo-wide grep confirmed no third caller of either RPC exists anywhere outside `api/election-day/session.ts` itself. The fail-closed `SERVER_ERROR` path for a DB/RPC error on either bucket registration is unchanged.

**Quality**: `npx tsc -b` clean; `npx eslint`/`prettier` clean on the one touched file; `git diff --check` clean; scope confirmed to exactly one file touched (`api/election-day/session.ts`) plus this documentation update - the new migration file's own content and SHA-256 are unchanged, Migration 55 unchanged, all 15 protected scripts unchanged.

**Status: rate-limit threshold correction implemented and verified locally. Still NOT applied to Production (no SQL change exists to apply). NOT committed.**

### Follow-up (2026-08-26) - final adversarial code/security review found and closed one real gap: cross-workspace role assignment in `create_permission_user_v3`

A final, read-only adversarial review of the exact uncommitted diff (not the prior reports - the actual current file contents), cross-checked by one independent code-review subagent with no access to this session's prior conclusions, found the review otherwise sound (ACL, rate-limit ordering/concurrency, session/reauth actor+workspace+action binding, credential handling, Origin/CSRF posture, legacy compatibility all confirmed correct by direct reading) but returned **BLOCKED** on one real gap, independently confirmed by both reviewers:

**Finding**: `election_day_create_permission_user_v3`'s role-existence check (`select 1 from election_day_roles where r.id = p_role_id`) verified only that the role *existed*, never that it belonged to the acting caller's own workspace. `election_day_roles.workspace_id` is a real, nullable column (Phase 1, `20260823020000`) - so once a second real workspace with its own custom role exists (an explicit near-future state of this same Multi-Tenant Program, not a hypothetical), a manager in Workspace A could pass an arbitrary `p_role_id` belonging to Workspace B and create a Workspace-A user holding Workspace-B's permission set. This directly contradicted the function's own stated design intent ("a manager creates users only within their own already-established workspace, by construction, never an arbitrary one") - true for `workspace_id`, false for `role_id`. Not exploitable today only because exactly one workspace exists system-wide right now. A secondary IP-spoofing concern the subagent raised (`x-forwarded-for` trust) was checked against Vercel's own current documentation (fetched live, `vercel.com/docs/headers/request-headers`: *"we currently overwrite the X-Forwarded-For header and do not forward external IPs. This restriction is in place to prevent IP spoofing"*) and found **not applicable** to this project's direct-to-Vercel deployment topology - not a real gap, correctly downgraded rather than acted on.

**Fix** (the only file changed): `supabase/migrations/20260826010000_multi_tenant_phase3a_session_rpcs.sql`, `election_day_create_permission_user_v3`'s role check is now `where r.id = p_role_id and r.workspace_id = v_actor_workspace_id` - deliberately **no** `or r.workspace_id is null` clause, per the explicit approved architectural decision that roles are workspace-scoped operational data with no global/shared-role model. A role in a different workspace, or one with `workspace_id IS NULL`, is rejected with the exact same `ROLE_NOT_FOUND` used for a genuinely nonexistent role - never a distinguishing message, so this never leaks whether the requested role exists in another workspace. `api/election-day/session.ts`, every other function in this migration, and Migration 55 are all untouched (SHA-256/diff-confirmed).

**Verified locally** (fresh `supabase db reset --local`, 56/56 migrations applied cleanly including the corrected function; all disposable fixtures cleaned up after, local stack stopped again afterward) - 8/8 checks passed: a caller in workspace A creating a user with a role also in workspace A succeeds, with the new user correctly receiving both workspace A and the requested role; the identical attempt with a role that instead belongs to workspace B is rejected with `ROLE_NOT_FOUND` and creates zero rows (no partial data); a role with `workspace_id IS NULL` is rejected the same way - confirming it is genuinely NOT treated as a shared/global role, closing exactly the gap found; `electionDay.manageUsers` is still required (a caller without it, even holding a valid session + proof for an otherwise-valid same-workspace role, still gets `FORBIDDEN`); username uniqueness is unchanged (a duplicate name still fails naturally via the table's existing unique constraint); the function remains fully inaccessible to `anon` (`insufficient_privilege`), matching its unchanged `service_role`-only ACL; and all 3 relevant legacy/`_v2` RPCs (`election_day_login`, `election_day_reauth`, `election_day_create_permission_user_v2`/`_delete_permission_user_v2`) still function, confirming nothing legacy was touched.

**Quality**: `git diff --check` clean; scope confirmed to exactly one file touched (the session-rpcs migration - only this one function's body and its two comment blocks changed); Migration 55 SHA-256 unchanged; `api/election-day/session.ts` SHA-256 unchanged (untouched, rate limits unchanged); all 15 protected scripts unchanged.

**Status: the cross-workspace role-assignment gap is closed and verified locally. Final review now PASSES. Still NOT applied to Production (no SQL exists to apply - Migration 55 is the only piece ever pushed there, and it doesn't contain this function). NOT committed.**

Live `pg_get_functiondef` pulled and compared for the three logic-critical functions (`login_v2`, `reauth_v3`, `create_permission_user_v3`) - byte-identical to the reviewed local source: 24h session expiry, 5-minute reauth expiry, and the workspace-scoped role check (`r.id = p_role_id and r.workspace_id = v_actor_workspace_id`, no `or r.workspace_id is null`) all confirmed live, verbatim.

**ACL finding**: 6 of 7 functions have exactly the intended ACL (`postgres`+`service_role` only, confirmed `anon`/`authenticated`/`PUBLIC` have zero EXECUTE on any of the 7 - the hard "not browser-executable" requirement is fully met for all 7). However, `election_day_verify_reauth_proof_v3` - designed as internal-only with **zero** grants to any role, including `service_role` - was found to actually carry `service_role` EXECUTE in Production. Root cause: this migration's ACL block for that one function only explicitly revokes from `public`/`anon`/`authenticated` (matching its "internal helper, no direct caller" design intent) but never explicitly revokes from `service_role` - and Production's own already-documented per-role default-privilege quirk (`pg_default_acl` for owner `postgres`/schema `public`/functions = `postgres,anon,authenticated,service_role` - re-confirmed present and unchanged this session) auto-grants `service_role` EXECUTE on every new function unless explicitly revoked. This gap was invisible in local Docker testing specifically because local replicas do not carry Production's default-privilege configuration (the same local/Production disparity the original Backfill-RPC incident and CLAUDE.md's Permanent Engineering Guardrail already document) - local testing correctly showed zero grant, Production did not.

**Severity assessment, not glossed over**: NOT browser-exploitable (only `service_role` gained the extra access, and no browser ever holds that credential) and NOT a novel mistake - the same exact pattern (revoke from `public`/`anon`/`authenticated` only, no explicit `service_role` revoke) already exists, unflagged until now, on the legacy `election_day_verify_reauth_proof` this function was directly modeled on (re-confirmed live: it too carries `service_role` EXECUTE despite the same "internal-only" design intent). Still a real, confirmed deviation from this function's own stated design ("Not granted to any role, including `service_role`") and from the strict, explicit-everything discipline this whole initiative has otherwise held itself to - not something to quietly accept. **Required follow-up, not yet authorized or performed**: a small, separate, later corrective migration adding exactly `revoke execute on function public.election_day_verify_reauth_proof_v3(bytea, bytea, text) from service_role;` - mirroring the exact shape of the original `20260824020000` Backfill-RPC ACL hotfix precedent.

**Legacy safety**: all 6 legacy functions (`election_day_login`, `election_day_reauth`, `election_day_verify_reauth_proof`, `election_day_revoke_reauth_proof`, `election_day_create_permission_user_v2`, `election_day_delete_permission_user_v2`) re-confirmed present with byte-identical ACLs to the pre-apply snapshot - zero touched by this migration.

**NULL-workspace user gate**: current count = **0**. Still required immediately before any future Session/login frontend cutover (re-check at that time, not assumed from this snapshot) - the legacy `_v2` create-user paths remain live and still omit `workspace_id`, so this can still go non-zero before cutover.

**Design**: a single new migration, `supabase/migrations/20260824010000_multi_tenant_phase2_historical_backfill_rpc.sql`, adds one RPC - `election_day_backfill_historical_workspace(p_auth_user_id, p_workspace_name, p_election_end_at, p_owner_name, p_owner_phone, p_owner_email)` - that atomically creates the one `election_workspaces` row, links the first `election_owners` row to an Auth identity already created out-of-band, and backfills `workspace_id` onto every pre-existing legacy row across all 12 Phase-1 tables (including the settings singleton). Idempotency-guarded: a fixed-key `pg_advisory_xact_lock` plus an existence check raises `HISTORICAL_WORKSPACE_ALREADY_EXISTS`/`OWNER_AUTH_USER_ALREADY_LINKED` rather than ever creating a second workspace/Owner. **REVOKEd from PUBLIC, granted only to `service_role`** - the first service_role-only RPC in the project (every other Election Day RPC is anon/authenticated-reachable); narrowly justified since this function creates platform-level workspace/Owner data with no legitimate anon/authenticated caller. `workspace_id` stays nullable everywhere - no Phase 3 tightening. No RLS policy change, no existing-RPC change, no `auth.*` table write, no hardcoded UUID anywhere in the migration.

**A real bug was found and fixed via live testing before finalizing**: the function was first written `SECURITY INVOKER` (reasoning: "only service_role calls it, and service_role already bypasses RLS"). Tested live against a disposable database, it failed immediately with `42501 permission denied` the moment it tried to touch `election_day_settings` - direct SQL inspection confirmed `service_role` holds only `REFERENCES`/`TRIGGER`/`TRUNCATE` on these tables, **not** `SELECT`/`INSERT`/`UPDATE` - bypassing RLS is unrelated to holding base table grants. Fixed to `SECURITY DEFINER` (owned by `postgres`, matching this project's own established pattern for RPCs that must write to zero-grant tables, e.g. `election_day_import_voters_v2`) - re-tested, passed cleanly. This would have failed identically against Production had it not been caught here.

**Settings-conversion scope decision (documented, not silently narrowed)**: the task asked to "convert the singleton settings into the workspace-scoped model." Before implementing, `SupabaseElectionDayApi.getElectionDayDeadline`/`setElectionDayDeadline` were checked directly and found to query `.eq("id", true)` - a hard dependency on `election_day_settings`'s existing `id boolean primary key default true` structure. Restructuring that PK (the "real" per-workspace conversion Phase 1's own migration comments flagged as Phase 2's eventual job) would break this existing frontend/RPC contract, which this phase must not change. Scoped decision: the PK/structure is left completely untouched; "conversion" for this one historical workspace means the single existing settings row is claimed by (gets `workspace_id` set to) the historical workspace, exactly like every other legacy row - no data loss, no structural change. A true per-workspace settings table (supporting more than one workspace, each with its own row) is explicitly deferred to a later, dedicated migration once a second real workspace is ever approved.

**Auth/compensation orchestration**: `scripts/lib/historicalBackfillOrchestration.ts` (pure, framework-agnostic - generates a strong random temporary password via `crypto.randomBytes(32)`, calls `supabaseAdmin.auth.admin.createUser`, then calls the RPC; on any RPC failure - including the idempotency guard rejecting a retry - compensates by calling `supabaseAdmin.auth.admin.deleteUser` on the just-created Auth user before rethrowing). The temporary password is returned exactly once in the orchestration's in-memory result for a future real caller to relay to the Owner - never logged, never written to any table.

**Pre-commit hardening pass (same day)**: a deployable placeholder endpoint (`api/admin/backfill-historical-workspace.ts`, a Vercel Server Function guarded only by a header-secret placeholder) was reviewed and rejected as unacceptable to commit before real Platform Owner Auth/MFA/authorization exists. **Removed entirely, along with the whole `api/` directory** - the orchestration module was relocated to `scripts/lib/historicalBackfillOrchestration.ts` (this project's own established convention for internal server-side modules, matching `scripts/lib/productionTestSafety.ts`), deliberately outside any `api/` path so it cannot become a Vercel route by accident. No replacement endpoint or other temporary production-facing auth shortcut was added. A real Vercel endpoint will be added only once the approved Platform Owner authentication/authorization layer exists.

**SECURITY DEFINER final security gate - reviewed and empirically proven, live, against a disposable database**: `proacl` for the function shows exactly `{postgres=X/postgres,service_role=X/postgres}` - no PUBLIC entry. `has_function_privilege` confirmed: service_role `true`, anon `false`, authenticated `false`. Real HTTP proof (not just SQL-level): a direct REST call to the RPC using the disposable project's anon key returned `401`/`42501 permission denied`; a real signed-up test user's `authenticated`-role JWT returned `403`/`42501 permission denied`. **Follow-up hardening pass (same day)**: per current Supabase/Postgres SECURITY DEFINER guidance, `search_path` was tightened further from `SET search_path = public` to **`SET search_path = ''`** (empty) - a caller still cannot override it regardless of their session `search_path`, and now nothing beyond `pg_catalog` (always implicit-first, per documented core Postgres behavior) resolves without explicit schema-qualification. Every table reference was already schema-qualified (`public.election_workspaces` etc.) and needed no change. The built-in function calls `hashtext`/`jsonb_build_object`/`length` were made explicitly `pg_catalog.`-qualified for full auditability (not because they were exploitable - they weren't, `pg_catalog` is always searched first regardless - but so nothing in the function relies on any implicit resolution at all). **A real, live-tested finding, not assumed**: an initial attempt to also qualify `trim(...)` as `pg_catalog.trim(...)` failed live against a disposable database with `function pg_catalog.trim(text) does not exist` - diagnosed directly (`pg_proc` inspection): plain `trim(x)` is SQL-standard `TRIM(...)` parser grammar, not a real function call, which Postgres rewrites internally to `btrim` before any name resolution happens - there is no function literally named `trim` in `pg_catalog` (only `btrim/1,2`), so `pg_catalog.trim(...)` can never exist and `trim(...)` is left correctly unqualified (it was never resolved via `search_path` in the first place, so there was nothing to harden there). Fixed and re-tested clean - full functional/schema/replay suite re-run end to end after the fix, all green (see below); this incidentally also proved the Auth-compensation path correctly cleans up even on a genuinely unexpected DB error, not just the deliberate idempotency-guard rejection, since the first (pre-fix) test run's Auth user was found correctly deleted by the orchestration's own catch block. Live re-verification post-fix: `proconfig` shows `{"search_path=\"\""}`, `prosecdef = t`, grants unchanged; anon REST call → `401`/`42501`; a real signed-up user's `authenticated` JWT → `403`/`42501`, both re-proven over actual HTTP identically to before.

Confirmed by inspection: the function signature has **no `p_workspace_id` parameter at all** - the workspace a row is backfilled into always comes from `v_workspace_id`, a local variable populated only from the function's own `INSERT ... RETURNING id`, never from any caller input, so no client-supplied identifier can ever redirect the operation to another workspace. The idempotency guard (fixed-key advisory lock + existence check) re-verified fail-closed via a real second orchestration run. The function structurally cannot become a general-purpose workspace/Owner creation primitive: it takes no workspace_id parameter, always creates at most one workspace ever (refuses outright once one exists), and always targets every legacy NULL row across all 12 tables as one fixed unit - there is no parameter or code path for creating a second workspace, or for scoping to an arbitrary existing workspace_id.

## INCIDENT (2026-08-24) - live Production privilege-escalation gap found and hotfixed

**Found**: post-apply verification of `20260824010000` queried Production's live `pg_proc.proacl` directly and found `election_day_backfill_historical_workspace`'s grants were `{postgres=X/postgres,anon=X/postgres,authenticated=X/postgres,service_role=X/postgres}` - **`anon` and `authenticated` both held EXECUTE**, contradicting the intended service_role-only design and every local disposable verification (which consistently showed service_role-only). Work stopped immediately per the standing failure-rule instruction - no Backfill was run, no fix was improvised in that turn.

**Root cause, confirmed via direct read-only inspection of Production's own `pg_default_acl` (not simulated/assumed)**: this specific hosted project carries a per-role default-privilege entry (role `postgres`, schema `public`, `objtype='f'`) that auto-grants `EXECUTE` on every _newly created_ function to `postgres`/`anon`/`authenticated`/`service_role` - a legacy "auto-expose new entities" default-privilege configuration this project's disposable local replicas don't have (the local Supabase CLI's own `config.toml` documents an "auto_expose_new_tables" legacy-vs-new-cloud-default split, confirming this is a known, real platform behavior difference, not a fluke). `REVOKE ... FROM PUBLIC` (what the original migration did) never touches these individually-named-role default grants - only an explicit `REVOKE ... FROM anon`/`FROM authenticated` does. **Verified no exploitation occurred**: Production's `election_workspaces`/`election_owners`/`platform_owners` all remained `0` rows throughout, and `auth.users` had `0` total rows in this project at the time of discovery (no real Supabase Auth identity existed that could have satisfied the Owner `auth_user_id` foreign key, so the gap - while real and live - had no actual attacker capable of exploiting it in practice).

**Hotfix**: `supabase/migrations/20260824020000_multi_tenant_phase2_backfill_rpc_acl_hotfix.sql` - four targeted statements only (`REVOKE EXECUTE ... FROM PUBLIC/anon/authenticated`, `GRANT EXECUTE ... TO service_role`) against the exact function signature. No function body/definition change, no `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` change (fixing the underlying legacy default-privilege configuration globally is a separate, larger, not-yet-approved change, deliberately out of scope for this narrow hotfix).

**Verified locally (disposable project, full fresh replay of all 53 migrations clean)**: the bug was faithfully _reproduced_ first (set the same default-privilege entry Production has, dropped and truly re-created the function to trigger it, confirmed `has_function_privilege` true for anon/authenticated) - then the hotfix was applied and confirmed to close it exactly: `has_function_privilege` service_role=true/anon=false/authenticated=false, `proacl` back to `{postgres=X/postgres,service_role=X/postgres}`. Real HTTP proof: anon → `401`/`42501`, a real signed-up user's `authenticated` JWT → `403`/`42501`. **Function definition hash (`md5(pg_get_functiondef(oid))`) confirmed byte-identical before and after the hotfix** - `SECURITY DEFINER`/`search_path=''`/business logic genuinely untouched. Full functional regression (happy path, retry/idempotency, Auth-compensation, all-12-table data integrity, settings preservation, credential non-persistence) re-run clean against the post-hotfix function - service_role invocation still works correctly. New schema regression `smoke-multi-tenant-phase2-acl-hotfix-schema.ts` (18/18). RLS policy count (6) and function count (55) unchanged. `tsc -b`/lint/`git diff --check` clean. Disposable project fully torn down. Main local `kolbox` stack and all 15 protected scripts untouched throughout.

**Hotfix applied to Production (2026-08-24) - INCIDENT CLOSED, reviewer-verified.** Post-apply verification against Production itself, not just the local disposable proof above: migrations `53/53` local==remote, zero drift; `proacl = {postgres=X/postgres,service_role=X/postgres}` (no PUBLIC entry); `has_function_privilege` - anon `false`, authenticated `false`, service_role `true`; a real anonymous HTTP call to the RPC returned `401`/`42501 permission denied`; function-definition hash unchanged (`SECURITY DEFINER`/`search_path=''`/business logic byte-identical before and after); `SECURITY DEFINER` and `search_path=''` both confirmed unchanged. Production data confirmed unaffected throughout: `election_workspaces`/`election_owners`/`auth.users` all `0` rows; all 12 Phase-1 tables still show zero non-null `workspace_id` anywhere - **no historical Backfill has run, and no real workspace/Owner/Auth account exists yet.** Git `HEAD = 91b5f76`, `origin/master...master = 0/0`; all 15 protected scripts unchanged.

**Follow-up authenticated-role runtime proof (same day)**: a synthetic-JWT HTTP test as a genuine `authenticated` caller was evaluated and determined **not safely possible** without either reusing an existing credential (explicitly prohibited) or obtaining Production's JWT signing material through a new, not-yet-authorized channel (this project uses the newer `sb_publishable_`/`sb_secret_` key format, under which the signing key is not necessarily exportable to the project owner at all) - so no such test was attempted, none was faked, and none is claimed. Closure instead rests on the SQL-level evidence above (`has_function_privilege` plus a direct `authenticated`-role membership check confirming zero indirect/inherited grant path), which exercises the identical authorization check PostgREST itself performs for a real HTTP request - accepted by the reviewer as sufficient, on the record, rather than being self-declared closed by the agent.

**Status: CLOSED.** The Phase 2 migration and its ACL hotfix are both live and verified correct on Production. The historical Backfill itself has not run - Production remains exactly as documented in the PHASE 1 CLOSED entry (12 tables, every row's `workspace_id` still `NULL`, zero workspaces, zero Owners, zero Auth accounts). Planning and execution of the real historical Backfill (workspace `מודיעין`) is a separate, not-yet-authorized next step.

## 2026-08-24 — Phase 2 Backfill Tooling (production runner + crash-recovery + post-flight) - implemented and committed LOCALLY ONLY

**Phase 2 historical Backfill tooling is implemented locally and is awaiting later Production execution authorization.** This is the operator-facing tooling that will run the actual historical Backfill against Production (the RPC + ACL hotfix themselves have been live since the INCIDENT section above) - it has not been used yet. **The historical Production Backfill has NOT run. No real historical workspace, Owner, or Auth user exists anywhere in Production** - state is unchanged from the INCIDENT section's closing status.

**Scope**: `scripts/run-historical-backfill-production.ts` (the human-run-only TTY production runner - identity hard gate, full pre-flight, password-before-mutation flow, crash-recovery receipt writing), `scripts/lib/historicalBackfillOrchestration.ts` (Auth-create → RPC → mandatory A/B/C reconciliation on any error), `scripts/lib/backfillTarget.ts` (the single fixed source of the approved target - workspace `מודיעין` / owner נחום שניר / `simofe8@gmail.com` - plus `assertReceiptMatchesApprovedTarget`, the "receipt is never authority" guard), `scripts/lib/backfillReceipt.ts` (non-secret crash-recovery receipt, strict phase ordering, forbidden-secret scanning), `scripts/lib/backfillPreflight.ts`/`backfillPostflight.ts`/`backfillReconciliationRead.ts`/`productionIdentity.ts`/`supabaseCliQuery.ts`, `scripts/recover-historical-backfill.ts` (strictly read-only recovery reporting - no `deleteUser`, no `createClient`, no RPC, no retry), `scripts/verify-historical-backfill-postflight.ts` (post-flight verifier, only advances the receipt to `POSTFLIGHT_VERIFIED` once every live check passes), and 3 test suites (`smoke-multi-tenant-phase2-backfill-safety.ts`, `-recovery.ts`, `-functional.ts`).

**A critical target-metadata-trust defect was found in a dedicated review pass and fixed before commit**: `backfillRecovery.ts` and `verify-historical-backfill-postflight.ts` were using the _receipt's own_ `ownerEmail`/`workspaceName`/`electionEndAtIso` as the basis for their supposedly-independent live-identity queries and pass/fail comparisons, instead of the fixed `backfillTarget.ts` constants - meaning a hand-edited or corrupted receipt could in principle have redirected recovery/verification at a different identity. Fixed via a new `assertReceiptMatchesApprovedTarget(receipt)` guard (in `backfillTarget.ts`) that both call sites now invoke first, hard-stopping before any live query on any mismatch; every subsequent query/comparison uses the fixed constants directly, never the receipt's copies. Proven live (real disposable Supabase project, not just synthetic): 33/33 pure tamper-coverage checks (each of `productionProjectRef`/`ownerEmail`/`workspaceName`/`electionEndAtIso`/`ownerName`/`ownerPhone` individually, via a spy that fails the test if any live query is invoked) plus 33/33 live recovery-scenario checks (`NOTHING_CREATED`/`PREFLIGHT_HARD_STOP`/`IDENTITY_MISMATCH_HARD_STOP`/`BRANCH_B_ORPHAN_CANDIDATE`/`BRANCH_A_LIKELY_COMMITTED`), all re-run and passing after the fix.

**`npm run typecheck:phase2-backfill` is the required TypeScript gate for this tooling** (`tsc -p scripts/tsconfig.phase2-backfill.json`, an explicit `files` list of exactly the Phase 2 files above, mirroring `tsconfig.node.json`'s compiler options including `strict`/`erasableSyntaxOnly`). **Root `npx tsc -b` does NOT typecheck anything under `scripts/` at all** (its project references cover only `src/` and `vite.config.ts`) **and must never be cited as typecheck proof for any file in `scripts/`, including this tooling.** A full-`scripts/` typecheck attempt found **9 pre-existing legacy files fail** (`harness/main.tsx`, `smoke-coordinator-allocation.ts`, `smoke-election-day-scope.ts`, `smoke-follow-up-status.ts`, `smoke-non-voting-reason-report.ts`, `smoke-permission-ui.ts`, `smoke-production-test-safety.ts`, `smoke-reminder-lifecycle.ts`, `smoke-role-catalog.ts`) - unrelated pre-existing type-shape drift, **left untouched, remaining out-of-scope technical debt**, not fixed as part of this work.

**Remaining pre-Production-execution requirement**: CLI-level end-to-end verification of `run-historical-backfill-production.ts`/`recover-historical-backfill.ts`/`verify-historical-backfill-postflight.ts` as actual invoked CLI processes (via a linked disposable project) has not been done - only their underlying library functions have been proven live so far. This remains required before the real Production execution, separately from this local-commit gate.

## 2026-08-23 — Coordinator Allocation: link/relink suggestion UI removed - since committed (`f475f8b`) and shipped to Production, including authenticated manual smoke (see "## Production" above)

A read-only investigation (requested separately from the removal itself) found the coordinator-row action/banner "קשר לאחראי הזה" ("link to this coordinator") - shown under every coordinator in `/election-day/files` → `ניהול הקצאות` - had become permanent, purpose-less noise in normal operation. Root cause, traced end-to-end: `linked_assignment_name` (added Phase 3, commit `8d7ce3d`, 2026-08-13) was originally a manual, explicit-only bridge for a coordinator whose `display_name` a manager typed by hand, letting them claim voters that had already arrived from Excel under a matching raw string. The 2026-08-20 coordinator-import auto-sync (`685d729`) then started creating every coordinator's `display_name` **already equal** to the matching raw Excel string - so the suggestion's trigger condition (`linkedAssignmentName === null && a raw voter matches the current display_name`) became trivially true for every auto-synced coordinator, permanently, since `isVoterOwnedByCoordinator` already counts that voter via `display_name` alone with no link needed. The two 2026-08-13/2026-08-20 features were never reconciled. The suggestion's only genuine (if narrow) use was preserving voter-linkage across a pre-activity rename during Setup - a workflow the product no longer uses.

**Product decision (approved)**: responsibility changes now go through `add new coordinator → transfer relevant voters/assignments → end/remove the previous coordinator`, never a rename-in-place. The manual link suggestion is therefore unnecessary in normal UI. **UI cleanup only** - remove the suggestion banner (both its "link" and "relink" variants, which share one trigger heuristic and one handler) from both Setup and Live; keep `linked_assignment_name`, the DB schema, every migration, and the `link`/`relink`/`unlink` RPC/API capability fully intact for any existing or future stored link. The standalone "בטל קישور" (unlink) action and the "מקושר ל-..." badge were deliberately **kept** - they manage/display an _existing_ link (a different, still-supported concern) rather than suggesting a new one; removing them was never in scope and would have removed the only UI-level way to manage a pre-existing link.

**Changed** (4 source files, both `CoordinatorRosterEditor`-family consumers per CLAUDE.md's "Shared component/UX change checklist"): `useCoordinatorRowActions.ts` (removed `suggestionCount`/`showLinkSuggestion`/`showRelinkSuggestion`/`handleLinkClick` - all dead once both banners were removed from their only 2 call sites; `handleUnlinkClick` and the `linkedAssignmentName`-aware `assignedCount`/eligibility logic are untouched), `CoordinatorRow.tsx` (Setup) / `CoordinatorLiveRow.tsx` (Live) (removed the two banner JSX blocks + the now-unused `Link2` icon import from each; every other action - rename, phone, remove, rebalance, end, unlink - untouched), `election-day.constants.ts` (removed the now-dead `linkSuggestion`/`linkButton`/`relinkButton`/`confirm.linkTitle`/`confirm.linkSummary` text keys; kept `unlinkButton`/`linkedBadge`/`confirm.unlinkTitle`/`confirm.unlinkSummary`). **No backend/API/type/migration file touched** - `CoordinatorAction`'s `"link"|"relink"|"unlink"` union, `mockApi.ts`, `supabaseElectionDayApi.ts`, `coordinatorAllocationMapping.ts`, and every file under `supabase/migrations/` are byte-identical to before (confirmed via `git diff` against the prior commit touching zero files under `supabase/`).

**Verification (local)**: `tsc -b` clean, `npm run build` clean, targeted `eslint` on all 4 changed files (0 errors, only this repo's pre-existing CRLF warnings), `git diff --check` clean (line-ending notices only). Extended the existing hermetic `scripts/smoke-coordinator-add-form.mjs` (already covered both consumers for the add-form) with 2 new additive regression blocks recreating the exact pre-removal trigger condition (a coordinator with `linked_assignment_name = null` plus a raw-imported voter whose `coordinator` text matches its `display_name`) for both Setup and Live - 29/29 checks passed, 0 console/network errors, all mocked REST/RPC, 0 real Supabase data touched. The pure-logic `scripts/smoke-coordinator-allocation.ts` suite (ownership/eligibility/rebalance/end business logic, unaffected by this UI-only change) re-run for confidence: all checks passed. All 16 protected `scripts/drive-*`/`scripts/smoke-*` files confirmed untouched throughout.

## 2026-08-22 — Coordinator Management UX fix: הסר אחראי visibility + ביטול on add-coordinator mode - since committed (`5cd7a11`) and shipped to Production (see "## Production" above)

The user's own manual Production smoke of `5b7a0cc` (see "## Production" above) found two real UX issues in `CoordinatorRosterEditor.tsx`, both purely presentational - no DB/RPC/business-logic/eligibility-rule change:

1. **`הסר אחראי` undiscoverable**: the remove action was an icon-only trash button, tiny and easy to miss. Replaced with a labeled `Button` (`variant="danger" size="sm"`, `Trash2` icon + `הסר אחראי` text) on its own row below the coordinator name, visually separate from `העבר הקצאות`/`סיום פעילות` (which live in a completely different list/section of `CoordinatorAllocationLive.tsx`, never in the same row). **Eligibility logic is untouched** - still driven by the exact same `isEligibleForEditOrRemove` (`assignedCount === 0`) the icon already used. When blocked by currently-assigned voters, the button stays visible and disabled, with the existing `assignedVotersReason` text now shown right beside it. When blocked by participation/history (not client-computable, by design - see the component's own long-standing comment), the button remains enabled client-side exactly as before, and the server's `COORDINATOR_LOCKED` rejection surfaces via the existing toast, which already names `סיום פעילות` as the guidance - this pre-existing behavior was verified unchanged, not newly built.
2. **No way to cancel out of add-coordinator mode**: `CoordinatorRosterEditor` gained an optional `onCancelAdd` prop - when provided, a `ביטול` (`COMMON_TEXT.cancel`) button appears next to the add/save button; clicking it clears the local `name`/`phone` state and calls `onCancelAdd()`. `CoordinatorAllocationLive.tsx` (the only call site with a collapsible add-coordinator panel) passes `onCancelAdd={() => setShowRoster(false)}`, which unmounts `CoordinatorRosterEditor` entirely - its internal `useState` naturally resets, so reopening always starts with empty fields, no explicit "did I remember to clear everything" logic needed. `CoordinatorAllocationSetup.tsx` (the dedicated setup-wizard step, no panel to close) does not pass the prop, so its UI is unchanged - no `ביטול` shown there, by design.

**Architecture decision (approved, 2026-08-22): the client stays deliberately blind to historical coordinator participation - no new RPC, migration, DB query, per-coordinator history fetch, or `hasParticipated` field.** A follow-up request asked whether `הסר אחראי` could be pre-disabled for a coordinator blocked by participation/history, the same way it already is for one blocked by currently-assigned voters. Investigated before touching any code: `election_day_coordinator_operations`/`election_day_coordinator_operation_items` (the only authoritative source, read by the server's `election_day_coordinator_participated()` guard) are RLS-locked with **zero grants to `anon`/`authenticated`** - confirmed live against local Docker (`42501 permission denied for table ...` on both, via the app's own anon key). `Coordinator.status` is NOT a valid proxy: its own type comment ("never deleted once it has participated - `status` flips to `ended` instead") is stale against the current RPC, which checks participation independently of `status` - an **active** coordinator can have real participation (fully rebalanced away to 0 remaining voters, never formally ended), exactly what the `UXFIX0822_history` fixture demonstrates. The one existing signal that IS already granted to `anon`/`authenticated` - `election_day_coordinator_participated(uuid)` itself - is not called anywhere in the frontend today, and calling it per-coordinator on render would add N new DB round-trips per roster load, precisely the cost `CoordinatorRosterEditor.tsx`'s own long-standing comment already weighed and rejected for this "rare edge case." **Decision: no code change.** `הסר אחראי` stays visible+enabled for a participation-blocked coordinator until clicked; the server's `COORDINATOR_LOCKED` rejection (existing toast, already names `סיום פעילות`) remains the sole detection point for that one case - unchanged, not a regression, a deliberate scope boundary.

**Follow-up (2026-08-22): `הסר אחראי` relocated out of the add-coordinator panel, into the main `אחראים` table.** Manual review found item 1 above's placement conceptually wrong: `הסר אחראי` lived inside the panel opened by `➕ הוסף אחראי`, but adding a coordinator and managing an existing one are different actions. Refactored (component split, no eligibility/business-logic change): a new `useCoordinatorRowActions` hook now owns all per-coordinator state/handlers (rename, phone, remove, Excel-assignment link/relink/unlink) previously inlined in `CoordinatorRosterEditor.tsx`'s `<ul>` map - consumed by two presentational components, `CoordinatorRow.tsx` (the flex-column list, still used by `CoordinatorAllocationSetup.tsx`'s dedicated coordinators step, unchanged there) and the new `CoordinatorLiveRow.tsx` (the day-of view's column-aligned grid row). `CoordinatorAllocationLive.tsx`'s main table now renders `CoordinatorLiveRow` per coordinator - `הסר אחראי` sits in the same actions cell as `העבר הקצאות`/`סיום פעילות`, and rename/phone/link management moved into that same row (no longer reachable only via the add panel). `CoordinatorRosterEditor.tsx` gained a `showExistingCoordinators` prop (default `true`, unchanged for Setup); the Live view's add panel now passes `showExistingCoordinators={false}`, so `➕ הוסף אחראי` opens ONLY the name/phone/add/`ביטול` form - no existing-coordinator controls exposed by that action. `ביטול` behavior (item 2 above) is fully unchanged. Eligibility rules, RPC calls, and text constants are byte-identical to before - this was a pure component/layout refactor.

**Verification (local Docker only)**: targeted `eslint` on the 3 touched files (0 errors, only pre-existing CRLF warnings), `npx tsc -b` clean, `npm run build` clean, `git diff --check` clean (line-ending-normalization notices only, same as every other file in this repo). Live Playwright verification against local Docker (disposable `UXFIX0822`-tagged fixtures: one coordinator with 0 assignments/no history, one with an assigned voter, one with real participation/history via a direct `coordinator_operations` row) at desktop 1440px and mobile 390px, 34/35 automated checks passed - the one "failure" is an expected `400` network-log entry from deliberately clicking the blocked history coordinator's `הסר אחראי` to prove the server correctly rejects it, not a real error. Confirmed: `הסר אחראי` visible+enabled for the eligible coordinator, visible+disabled with the reason text for the assigned-voters coordinator, visible+enabled-but-server-rejected (with toast guidance to `סיום פעילות`) for the history coordinator and the coordinator was NOT removed; `ביטול` visible when the add form opens, closes the form immediately on an empty form, discards partially-typed name+phone and reopens empty, and never issues an RPC call (the discarded name never appears anywhere); zero horizontal overflow at either width; phone add/edit unaffected (regression-checked). The `UXFIX0822` fixtures were **deliberately left in place** (not cleaned up) so the user's own visual walkthrough can inspect all three states live - to be removed after the user confirms, following this project's standard exact-id disposable-fixture convention.

**Row-move verification (local Docker only, same fixtures)**: targeted `eslint` on the 5 touched/new files (0 errors, only pre-existing CRLF warnings), `npx tsc -b` clean, `npm run build` clean, `git diff --check` clean. Live Playwright re-verification at desktop 1440px and mobile 390px, 38/39 checks passed (the 1 "failure" is the same expected deliberate-`400` case as above, not a real error): main-table rows show `העבר הקצאות`/`סיום פעילות`/`הסר אחראי` together with the exact same enabled/disabled/reason states as before; exactly 3 `הסר אחראי` buttons exist even while the add panel is open (none inside the panel itself); `ביטול` + empty/partial-cancel/reopen-empty all still work; phone editing works from the main table row; zero horizontal overflow at both widths.

**Visual redesign (2026-08-22): `הסר אחראי` restyled from solid red to a restrained `danger-outline` treatment.** The user reviewed the moved button live and rejected its `variant="danger"` styling (solid saturated `bg-opponent` red pill) as "too loud... childish... not modern/premium/professional" - functionality and placement were confirmed correct, only the visual treatment needed to change. A design-review pass (general-purpose subagent, research-only) found the app already has an established "soft destructive" pattern used in 6+ existing delete affordances elsewhere (a muted slate icon that only fills with `opponent-soft`/`opponent` on hover) - `variant="danger"` was the one outlier. Added a new, reusable `"danger-outline"` variant to `src/components/ui/Button.tsx`'s shared variant map (`bg-white text-slate-500 ring-1 ring-slate-200`, hover fills `bg-opponent-soft text-opponent ring-opponent/30`, active `bg-rose-100` - the same shared `disabled:opacity-50` and `focus-visible:outline-primary-500` from the base `Button` component, left as-is, apply unchanged) and swapped `variant="danger"` → `variant="danger-outline"` on both `CoordinatorLiveRow.tsx`'s and `CoordinatorRow.tsx`'s remove buttons - no placement/size/text/eligibility change, purely a token/variant swap in a shared component. Verified live: default state now white/outlined (not solid red), hover measurably shifts to `rgb(255, 228, 230)` (`opponent-soft`) on both desktop and mobile, disabled state stays visibly muted, 13/13 automated checks + 0 console/network errors, `tsc -b`/`npm run build`/`git diff --check` all clean.

**User approval + fixture cleanup (2026-08-22): all of the above manually reviewed and approved live.** The user confirmed, in order: the row-move placement, the new `danger-outline` visual treatment, the assigned-voter disabled state + reason, the server-authoritative history/login-dependency blocking behavior (unchanged, by the architecture decision above), and the full `ביטול` cancel/discard/reopen-empty behavior. The 6 disposable `UXFIX0822`-tagged local fixtures (3 coordinators, 1 assigned voter, 1 `PermissionUser`, 1 `coordinator_operations` row) were then removed by exact id, each deletion preceded by an explicit ownership check (the operation row's `executed_by_name_snapshot = 'UXFIX0822 test setup'` and `subject_coordinator_id` both confirmed exclusive to this batch before deletion; 0 `election_day_coordinator_operation_items` rows referenced any of the 3 coordinator ids). Verified after: 0 `UXFIX0822` rows remain in any table (coordinators/voters/permission*users/operations/reauth_proofs), and unrelated local data is exactly unchanged - 1 real voter (unchanged), the same 3 pre-existing `PermissionUser` accounts (`אחראי בדיקה`/`בודק ידני`/`מנהל*בדיקה_ידנית`), 7 pre-existing `coordinator_operations` rows (8 total before cleanup, minus the 1 owned by this batch).

**Status: committed as `5cd7a11` and shipped to Production (2026-08-22) - see "## Production" above.** Files touched/added: `src/features/election-day/CoordinatorRosterEditor.tsx`, `src/features/election-day/CoordinatorAllocationLive.tsx`, `src/features/election-day/election-day.constants.ts` (new `roster.removeButton` text key), `src/features/election-day/useCoordinatorRowActions.ts` (new), `src/features/election-day/CoordinatorRow.tsx` (new), `src/features/election-day/CoordinatorLiveRow.tsx` (new), `src/components/ui/Button.tsx` (new `danger-outline` variant). No migration, no RPC change - a pure frontend patch. All 16 protected scripts untouched. The rest of this section is kept as-written for the local-verification history it documents.
