New files `overdueReminderPopups.ts` (pure derivation: DUE and not `lastCallAttemptAt >= reminderAt`) and `OverdueReminderStack.tsx`; `useElectionDay.ts` gained `scopedContacts` in its return and lost the old toast-interval effect; rendered once at the `ElectionDayShell` level so it persists across all 7 screens. "Handled by call" reuses the existing `incrementCallAttempts` RPC's own `last_call_attempt_at` write - no new column. "דחה" (postpone) reuses the existing `ReminderMenu` component as-is, wired to the existing `setReminder`/`setReminderAt`. Manager exclusion uses the resolved role's existing `scopeType` field (`"assigned_to_me"` required, same signal `electionDayScope.ts` already uses elsewhere) - not a role name/id check, so it generalizes to any future unrestricted role, not just today's "manager." A real bug was found and fixed during verification (not a pre-existing issue): with several cards expanded, one card's "דחה" dropdown could render underneath the next card in the list (a CSS stacking-context collision from stacking multiple `ReminderMenu` instances, never exercised before this feature existed) - fixed with a scoped `relative z-0 focus-within:z-30` on each card wrapper, without touching `ReminderMenu.tsx` itself.

Full local functional verification (disposable local-only Supabase accounts/contacts, created and deleted every round): manager sees no bar; a scoped user does; 7 overdue → bar reads 7, expands to 5 + "עוד 2"; oldest-first confirmed; a call decrements the count and removes that card; postpone decrements the count until the new time; the bar/count survives a full page reload and screen navigation; desktop (1440px) and mobile (390px) both verified via screenshots. Production verification was necessarily narrower - no Production data mutation or test account was permitted, so **authenticated in-app reminder behavior was not directly exercised live**; what _was_ verified against Production is that the exact code proven correct locally is what's actually deployed (bundle-hash match to the local build of `98fde6c`, plus a direct grep of the live bundle confirming the new bar-label strings/CSS classes are present and the old toast string is absent).

**Incidental, out-of-scope finding, explicitly not fixed** (flagged for whoever picks it up, see "Standing open items" below): several existing buttons across the contact modal and both new features (call/reschedule/close-reminder, using an arbitrary-value `bg-[#00a400]`/`bg-[#f59f00]` layered on `<Button>`'s default `variant="primary"`) render as plain primary/purple instead of their intended green/orange, because `cn()` (`src/lib/utils.ts`) is plain `clsx` with no Tailwind-merge conflict resolution. Confirmed pre-existing (present in the shipped contact modal itself, not introduced by either feature above) - deliberately not touched, since fixing it only in new code would make the new UI disagree with the old modal's colors for the same actions.

## Approved architectural decisions (do not change without explicit re-approval)

- **No protected roles.** Every role, including the three built-in ones, is fully editable/cloneable/deletable.
- **Protect the permission, not a role name.** "ניהול תפקידים והרשאות" (`electionDay.manageRolesAndPermissions`) must always be held by at least one user - enforced at the DB/RPC level in a future phase, not just client-side.
- **Deleting a role with assigned users is always blocked** - no auto-reassignment, no cascading user deletion. Enforced today at the DB level via `ON DELETE RESTRICT` (Phase 0).
- **"ניהול תפקידים והרשאות" is separate from "ניהול הרשאות משתמשים"** (`electionDay.manageUsers`) - two independent permissions.
- Role fields: name, description, **scope** (תחום עבודה: "כל אנשי הקשר" / "רק המוקצים לי" today, modeled as `scope_type` + reserved `scope_value` so a future dimension, e.g. geographic, is additive, not breaking). "Scope" as a word is never shown in the UI.
- "Clone role" is a required capability.
- Editing an existing user's role is out of scope (stays delete + recreate).
- **Core principle**: the system only knows permissions. `manager`/`operations`/`voting`/any future role are just named permission bundles - there is no "role type" concept in code.
- `legacy_role_key` was a temporary migration-bookkeeping anchor only (removed entirely in Phase 3, see below) - it never participated in any permission/scope decision itself, and was never shown in any UI or touched by role editing while it existed.
- Every migration wrapped in explicit `begin;`/`commit;` (the Supabase CLI pipelines a file's statements, it does not implicitly wrap them in a transaction).
- Rollout was 4 independent, individually testable/deployable/rollback-able phases - no big-bang cutover. All 4 are now shipped.

## Coordinator Delete ("הסר אחראי") - IMPLEMENTED and verified (local Docker only), safe edit+remove guard - uncommitted

Investigated adding a genuine `הסר אחראי` action to Allocation Management, distinct from `העבר הקצאות`/`סיום פעילות`. **Not implemented - correctly stopped before any DB/RPC/schema change, both times this was investigated (2026-08-21).**

**What already exists**: the `remove` action is already fully wired end-to-end at the application layer - `CoordinatorAction`'s TS type already includes `"remove"`, `toCoordinatorActionPayload` already maps it, `manageCoordinators()` already sends it to `election_day_manage_coordinators_v2`, and that RPC already has a `remove` branch (`elsif v_action_type = 'remove' then ... delete from election_day_coordinators ...`). No UI button calls it yet - that's the only missing piece at the application layer.

**Why it can't safely ship as-is**: the RPC's own safety gate for `remove` is `election_day_has_allocation_activity()` - a **global**, one-way, all-coordinators flag ("has ANY `election_day_coordinator_operations` row ever been written, for ANY coordinator"), not a per-coordinator check. This is the wrong predicate for the stated requirement in two directions at once: (1) it never checks whether voters are currently assigned to _this_ coordinator via `election_day_voters.coordinator` (a plain free-text column, no FK to `election_day_coordinators` by original design - confirmed by rereading `20260811100000_election_day_coordinator_nullable.sql`'s own comment) - a coordinator that received voters purely via CSV import/coordinator-sync (never through an allocation/rebalance/end RPC) could be deleted while still holding real assigned voters; (2) once the global flag trips even once, `remove` is permanently blocked for _every_ coordinator forever after, including one added by mistake seconds ago with zero history of its own - directly contradicting the stated "typical case: added by mistake, never used" requirement. **Verified this isn't hypothetical**: a direct read-only Production query (`select count(*) from election_day_coordinator_operations`) returned **1** - the global flag is already tripped in Production right now, so the existing `remove` action, if wired up today, would immediately and permanently fail with `COORDINATOR_LOCKED` for every coordinator, with no way to distinguish a genuinely-safe one.

**The correct predicate**, from the real architecture (not guessed): a coordinator is safe to physically delete iff **both** (a) `NOT election_day_coordinator_participated(coordinator_id)` - a **per-coordinator** boolean RPC that already exists and is already granted to `anon`/`authenticated` (used today for `link`/`unlink`/`relink` gating), checking whether this specific coordinator was ever a `from`/`to` in `election_day_coordinator_operation_items` or the `subject` of an `election_day_coordinator_operations` row; and (b) `NOT EXISTS (select 1 from election_day_voters v where v.coordinator = <this coordinator's display_name>)` - no voter currently free-text-matches this coordinator, covering the import-only-assignment gap (a) misses.

**Minimal proposed DB/RPC design** (not built, pending separate approval): a migration that `create or replace`s `election_day_manage_coordinators_v2`'s `remove` branch to swap its guard from the global `election_day_has_allocation_activity()` to the per-coordinator pair above (condition (a) already has a ready-made function; condition (b) is a one-line inline `EXISTS`, no new function needed) - same signature, same permission gate (`electionDay.manageCoordinatorAllocation` via reauth proof), no schema/column/FK/RLS change.

**Concurrency - re-investigated and resolved (2026-08-21, second pass)**: the earlier report left this as an open question, reasoning from `election_day_manage_coordinators_v2`'s ORIGINAL (20260813120000) definition, which indeed did not take the global `election_day_voter_allocation_mutation` advisory lock. That's now stale - rereading the migration history in full found `20260820000000_election_day_import_coordinator_sync.sql` (the Coordinator Sync checkpoint, already live in Production) **extended this exact function to also acquire that lock**, closing a different TOCTOU gap (the coordinator-sync-on-import step). The CURRENT live definition takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('election_day_voter_allocation_mutation'))` immediately after the auth/permission check and before the coordinator-row `FOR UPDATE` lock - the exact same lock, same position, every other allocation-mutation RPC (rebalance/initial-allocation/end/import) already uses. **This means the proposed `remove` fix needs no new lock at all** - a new voter-assignment `EXISTS` read added inside the `remove` branch would already run fully serialized against every concurrent business RPC, since none of them can be mid-transaction past this same advisory lock at the same time. No deadlock risk either: every RPC that touches this lock acquires it as a single global mutex _before_ any row-level lock, in the same relative order, every time - two competing calls always fully serialize at the advisory-lock step itself, never interleave their row-lock acquisition. **"Do not invent a separate lock" is satisfied by construction: the existing lock is already exactly right, already in place, nothing to add.**

**Error-code question** (reuse `COORDINATOR_LOCKED` for both the participation and voter-assignment cases, vs. a new distinct code e.g. `COORDINATOR_HAS_ASSIGNED_VOTERS`) is a minor product/copy decision, left open for whoever approves the migration.

**UI design** (described, not built - per the task's explicit "prepare the design, do not wire it to an unsafe RPC" instruction): a third `הסר אחראי` button on each active coordinator's row, visually distinct from `העבר הקצאות`/`סיום פעילות` (destructive styling); clicking it first checks the safe-delete predicate (client can already read `election_day_coordinator_participated` and the same `coordinatorStats.totalAssigned` it already has for every other roster row); if ineligible, a concise inline reason is shown and the manager is pointed at `העבר הקצאות`/`סיום פעילות` instead, no RPC call made; if eligible, a confirmation dialog requires typing the coordinator's exact name before the (then-fixed) RPC's `remove` action is sent.

**Identity-safety re-investigation (2026-08-21, third pass) - found a real gap, STOPPED before authoring the migration**: the task's mandatory pre-migration identity check asked three specific questions about `election_day_voters.coordinator = election_day_coordinators.display_name`, answered by rereading the actual schema/RPC bodies rather than assuming:

- **Display-name uniqueness**: only a **partial** unique index - `election_day_coordinators_active_display_name_key on (display_name) where status = 'active'`. At most one _active_ coordinator can hold a given name at any moment, but an _ended_ coordinator's name is free to be reused by a brand-new one - by design (`20260811100100`'s own comment: "ending and re-adding the same person next election day is never blocked by a stale row").
- **Rename behavior - the actual finding**: rename is a real, live, UI-wired feature (`CoordinatorRosterEditor.tsx`'s inline edit, `confirmEditClick` → `action: "edit"` → `election_day_manage_coordinators_v2`'s `edit` branch, `update election_day_coordinators set display_name = ... where id = ...`). **This `edit` never touches `election_day_voters.coordinator`** - confirmed by reading the full RPC body; only the three real allocation RPCs (`apply_initial_allocation_v2`/`rebalance_assignments_v2`/`end_coordinator_activity_v2`) write `election_day_voters.coordinator`, always stamping it with the acting coordinator's _display_name at that moment_. `edit`'s own guard is the same global `election_day_has_allocation_activity()` flag as `remove` - meaning rename is only reachable in the pristine window _before the first-ever allocation/rebalance/end operation in the whole session_, but that window is real and commonly hit: `election_day_sync_coordinators_from_voters()` (the automatic import-time coordinator backfill, confirmed by rereading its migration) auto-creates a coordinator entity for every distinct `voters.coordinator` string **without ever setting `linked_assignment_name`** and without writing any `election_day_coordinator_operations` row - so a coordinator auto-synced right after import, already the free-text owner of real assigned voters via raw string match, can still be renamed via `edit` at that point with zero participation recorded.
- **Identity reuse / stale-name ambiguity - the concrete unsafe sequence proven reachable**: (1) import lands voters with `coordinator = 'רחל'`; (2) the import's own sync auto-creates coordinator A, `display_name = 'רחל'`, `linked_assignment_name = null`, zero operations recorded; (3) before any real allocation ever runs anywhere in the session, a manager renames A via `edit` to `'שרה'` (unblocked - no activity yet); (4) the `'רחל'`-labeled voter rows are **not** updated by step 3 - `edit` never cascades to `election_day_voters`; (5) checking the proposed safe-delete predicate against A now - `NOT election_day_coordinator_participated(A.id)` → true (A genuinely never ran an allocation op); `NOT EXISTS (voters where coordinator = A.display_name)` → checks `coordinator = 'שרה'`, finds **zero** matches (the real assigned voters still say `'רחל'`) → **the predicate wrongly reports A as safe to delete**, even though A is the entity that owns those `'רחל'`-labeled voters in every way the rest of the app (dashboard breakdown, coordinator scoping) actually treats as "assignment." Deleting A at that point silently orphans the free-text label with no coordinator entity behind it, and a later coordinator that happens to reuse the name `'רחל'` (freely allowed once A is gone / once A never held it as `status = 'active'` under that name) would appear to "inherit" those voters with zero real relationship to A's history - exactly the ambiguity the task asked to rule out, and it is not ruled out.
- **Why this wasn't caught by the two earlier passes**: both prior investigations reasoned about `remove` in isolation (participation-check correctness, then lock/concurrency correctness) and never traced whether a _different_ action (`edit`) could invalidate `remove`'s own name-based predicate between the check and any future delete - the gap is at the seam between two actions, not inside `remove` itself.

**STOP condition met, per the task's own explicit instruction ("if the text-name relationship is not provably safe... STOP before creating any migration")**: the identity relationship is **not** provably safe for a "modify only the `remove` branch" fix, because closing the gap requires the _same_ per-coordinator "not currently assigned" predicate to also gate `edit` (rename) - a coordinator must not be renameable away from a name that still has voters currently matching it, or the exact stale-old-name scenario above stays open. That is a two-branch change, wider than the "CREATE OR REPLACE, modify only the `remove` behavior" scope this task pre-authorized - so it is reported here as an open design question, not built.

**Smallest professional solution** (schema/FK-redesign-free, as required): extend the identical `NOT election_day_coordinator_participated(id) AND NOT EXISTS (voters where coordinator = display_name)` gate to **both** `remove` and `edit` in the same `create or replace` of `election_day_manage_coordinators_v2` - renaming a coordinator that currently has zero free-text-matching voters is unaffected (the common case: fixing a typo moments after adding, before any voter has been linked/imported against it); renaming one that _does_ have currently-matching voters is blocked with a clear reason (e.g. reuse `COORDINATOR_LOCKED`, or a new `COORDINATOR_HAS_ASSIGNED_VOTERS`), forcing the manager to reassign/unlink first - eliminating the seam the unsafe sequence above depends on. No new lock needed (same reasoning as the second pass - the existing `election_day_voter_allocation_mutation` advisory lock already covers the whole action-processing loop, `edit` included). No schema/FK/RLS change of any kind.

**Status: NOT READY for migration approval as previously scoped ("remove-only")** - the per-coordinator predicate, minimal diff shape, and concurrency behavior are all correct and unchanged from the second pass, but the fix must additionally touch the `edit` branch to be genuinely safe; that's an explicit scope decision for whoever approves this (extend to both branches, or accept the residual gap and document it as a known limitation instead - both are legitimate calls, just not this session's to make unilaterally). **No code was written for this fix** - investigation and this design report only, all three times.

### Approved and implemented (2026-08-21, fourth pass) - the two-branch fix above, built exactly as scoped

The product decision above (extend the safe-delete predicate to `edit` too) was explicitly approved. Implemented, applied to local Docker only, fully verified - **not applied to Production, not committed**.

**Migration**: `supabase/migrations/20260821030000_election_day_coordinator_safe_edit_remove.sql` - `create or replace` of `election_day_manage_coordinators_v2`, same public signature, same permission/reauth gate, same `SECURITY DEFINER`/`search_path=''` hardening, same advisory-lock position (unchanged - the existing `election_day_voter_allocation_mutation` lock, already acquired before any row lock since 20260820000000, already covers the new checks since they run later in the same lock-held transaction; no new lock introduced). `add`/`link`/`relink`/`unlink` and the return contract are byte-for-byte unchanged.

- **`edit`**: now rejects with `DISPLAY_NAME_LOCKED` (single, reused code - the rename UX needs one combined reason, not two) if `election_day_coordinator_participated(id)` is true, OR if any voter currently matches the coordinator's _current_ `display_name` (read fresh inside the same transaction, before the rename). Both checks run before the existing `linked_assignment_name` collision check. Voter-name rewriting was explicitly NOT added - `edit` still only ever touches `election_day_coordinators.display_name`, exactly as before; the fix is entirely "block the rename," never "cascade the rename."
- **`remove`**: same two-condition guard, replacing the old global `election_day_has_allocation_activity()` flag, but reported as two DISTINCT codes so the UI can give a specific reason each: `COORDINATOR_LOCKED` (reused - participation/history, direct the manager to `סיום פעילות`) and a new `COORDINATOR_HAS_ASSIGNED_VOTERS` (currently-assigned voters, direct the manager to `העבר הקצאות`).
- Applied to local Docker via `npx supabase migration up --local` (clean, no errors); confirmed absent from the linked Production project (`npx supabase migration list --linked` shows an empty `remote` field for `20260821030000`, all 46 prior migrations still `local == remote`).

**Direct-RPC verification** (bypassing the UI entirely, real reauth proofs issued via the real `election_day_reauth` RPC, disposable data, all cleaned up after each scenario, `count = 0` confirmed):

- **Never-used coordinator**: rename succeeded, then remove succeeded, row gone.
- **Assigned, zero-history coordinator**: rename → `DISPLAY_NAME_LOCKED`; remove → `COORDINATOR_HAS_ASSIGNED_VOTERS`; coordinator row and the voter's `coordinator` string both provably unchanged after.
- **History-only coordinator** (real participation via an actual `apply_initial_allocation_v2` call, then every voter rebalanced away via a real `rebalance_assignments_v2` call, leaving 0 current voters but `election_day_coordinator_participated() = true`): rename → `DISPLAY_NAME_LOCKED`; remove → `COORDINATOR_LOCKED` - both correctly blocked despite zero current voters, proving the participation check (not just the voter-count check) is genuinely load-bearing.
- **Stale-name regression** (the exact originally-found unsafe sequence, reproduced deliberately): a never-participated coordinator with a real currently-assigned voter attempts rename - now correctly rejected (`DISPLAY_NAME_LOCKED`); display_name and the voter's coordinator string both confirmed unchanged - the gap that started this investigation is closed.
- **Regression** (same direct-RPC session): `add`, `link`, `unlink`, a real `apply_initial_allocation_v2` call, a real `rebalance_assignments_v2` call, and a real `end_coordinator_activity_v2` (transfer mode) call all completed correctly with no errors - none of the untouched branches regressed.

**Concurrency proof** (two genuinely concurrent local `psql` sessions, not a synthetic single-process test): session 1 opened a transaction and held the exact `election_day_voter_allocation_mutation` advisory lock for 4 seconds (`pg_sleep(4)`, simulating an in-progress assignment/rebalance); session 2 concurrently called `election_day_manage_coordinators_v2` with a `remove` action against an eligible coordinator. Session 2's call measurably blocked for the full 4 seconds (timed via wall-clock, not inferred) before proceeding and succeeding once session 1 committed - proving assignment/rebalance and remove fully serialize through the existing single mutex, exactly as the pre-implementation concurrency analysis predicted, no deadlock. The mirror case (renaming while a voter is concurrently being assigned to the old identity) is covered by the same structural guarantee rather than a second empirical race: because both the `edit` guard's voter-read and every allocation RPC's voter-write happen strictly after acquiring the same lock, in the same relative position, in every RPC, no interleaving is possible - only clean "rename-then-assign" or "assign-then-rename" orderings, both of which the scenarios above already prove are individually safe.

**UI** (`CoordinatorRosterEditor.tsx`, shared by both the Setup screen and the day-of "➕ הוסף אחראי" section in the live management view - both call sites now pass `allowRename allowRemove` (true), replacing the old blanket `allowRename={false} allowRemove={false}` in `CoordinatorAllocationLive.tsx`, whose original reason - "no reliable per-coordinator participation signal to hide them with" - is exactly what this fix provides): per-row eligibility computed client-side via `countVotersWithRawCoordinatorName(contacts, c.displayName) === 0` - the same free-text match the RPC's own guard uses, so "has assigned voters" is a certain, zero-round-trip signal. A row with assigned voters shows both buttons disabled (with a distinct blocked `aria-label`) and an inline reason (`text.assignedVotersReason` - "יש להעביר את ההקצאות תחילה"). The complementary "has participation/history" reason has no cheap client-side signal (no per-coordinator RPC call was added just to render row state, matching "frontend pre-checks are UX only") - that case leaves the buttons enabled and surfaces only if the RPC itself rejects the attempt, through the existing shared toast (`mapCoordinatorAllocationRpcErrorMessage`, extended with the new `COORDINATOR_HAS_ASSIGNED_VOTERS` code and refined `DISPLAY_NAME_LOCKED`/`COORDINATOR_LOCKED` message text pointing at `העבר הקצאות`/`סיום פעילות` respectively). Destructive confirmation (coordinator name shown, explicit confirm) is provided by this codebase's existing reauth-dialog-doubles-as-confirmation pattern (`ReauthCopy`, unchanged) - the same mechanism every other coordinator mutation already uses, not a new dialog. Buttons are additionally restricted to `c.status === "active"` rows (an ended coordinator's lifecycle is already closed via `סיום פעילות`, out of scope for this feature).

**Full local browser UI proof** (Playwright, disposable manager account + 2 disposable coordinators + 1 disposable voter, real login, real reauth dialog, cleaned up after, 13/13 checks): unused-coordinator row shows both buttons enabled; assigned-coordinator row shows both buttons disabled with the correct blocked `aria-label`s and the inline reason text; force-clicking the disabled remove button does nothing (coordinator count unchanged in the DB); removing the eligible coordinator shows the reauth/confirm dialog with the coordinator's name in its copy, completes, and the row is gone from the DB; a subsequent add + rename (proof now cached, no dialog shown) both succeed immediately - matches this app's established "dialog only on the cold-start action, immediate execution within the cached-proof window" behavior exactly, not a new pattern.

**Quality**: `npx tsc -b` clean; `npm run build` clean; targeted `eslint` on all 5 touched application files - 0 errors (pre-existing CRLF warnings only); `scripts/smoke-coordinator-allocation.ts` (not one of the 16 protected scripts) updated to match the new intentional `DISPLAY_NAME_LOCKED`/`COORDINATOR_LOCKED` message text and to add `COORDINATOR_HAS_ASSIGNED_VOTERS` - full pass after the update; `scripts/smoke-allocation-reauth-cutover.ts` full pass, confirming the reauth-proof cutover itself is untouched; `git diff --check` clean on every touched file; migration reviewed line-by-line against the prior verified definition (20260820000000) to confirm only the `edit`/`remove` branches actually changed; scope confirmed to exactly 5 modified application files + 1 modified non-protected smoke test + 1 new migration, all 16 protected scripts unchanged. Dashboard Reminders and the shared Layout fix were re-verified unaffected (reminder card renders correctly, 0 horizontal overflow across the 7 standard widths) with fresh disposable data.

**Status (superseded by the identity-invariant amendment below): IMPLEMENTED, verified against local Docker Supabase only. Not applied to Production, not committed.**

### Final coordinator identity invariant (2026-08-21, amended in place) - closes the login-identity gap, `20260821030000` is now the final definition

Two further read-only reviews (still 2026-08-21) found the above design, while correct for the `election_day_voters`-only relationship, was incomplete against the FULL identity picture: (1) the voter-assignment check only looked at `display_name`, never `linked_assignment_name` - inconsistent with `election_day_end_coordinator_activity_v2`'s own long-standing `v_source_names := array_remove(array[display_name, linked_assignment_name], null)` pattern, which treats both columns as equally valid "this coordinator's voters"; (2) `election_day_permission_users.name` (the completely separate login roster - no FK, no relationship of any kind to `election_day_coordinators`) is how `assigned_to_me` scoping actually works (`electionDayScope.ts`: `contacts.filter(c => c.coordinator === sessionUser.name)`) - renaming or deleting a coordinator whose name a live login depends on would silently blind that person to their own assignments, with zero error anywhere; (3) `add` never blocked reusing an ENDED coordinator's `display_name` (only checked active-active collision via the DB's partial unique index, and `linked_assignment_name` in any status) - a new active coordinator could be created sharing a name with an ended one that still has real voters, with no way to tell which row a shared voter-name string belongs to.

**Since `20260821030000` was still uncommitted and local-only, it was amended in place** (not layered under a third migration) to the final invariant:

- **`add`**: rejected if the proposed `display_name` matches ANY existing coordinator's `display_name` OR `linked_assignment_name`, in ANY status - closes the ended-name-reuse gap. Deliberately NOT checked against `election_day_permission_users` - a coordinator is routinely created before its matching login account exists.
- **`edit`**: allowed only if NOT participated, AND no voter matches the coordinator's current `display_name` OR `linked_assignment_name`, AND no `election_day_permission_users.name` matches either - all three failures raise the same `DISPLAY_NAME_LOCKED` code (one combined reason). A fourth, distinct check (unchanged in kind from before, now also covering `display_name` not just `linked_assignment_name`) blocks the NEW name colliding with any other coordinator's identity names, raising `COORDINATOR_NAME_COLLISION`.
- **`remove`**: the same three-condition safety guard, raising three DISTINCT codes: `COORDINATOR_LOCKED` (participation/history), `COORDINATOR_HAS_ASSIGNED_VOTERS` (currently-assigned voters), and a new `COORDINATOR_HAS_LOGIN_ACCOUNT` (a live login depends on this identity).
- `link`/`relink`/`unlink` **confirmed unchanged** - direct code review found they only ever write `linked_assignment_name`, which `electionDayScope.ts` never reads; no reason to expand their scope.

**Local Docker synchronization, explicit and proven** (per the task's own requirement not to assume editing the file reapplies it): the amended file's SQL was piped directly into local Docker via `psql` (`CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` is idempotent, no `db reset` needed); `pg_get_functiondef('election_day_manage_coordinators_v2'::regprocedure)` was then pulled from the live database and read in full - **line-for-line identical** to the file's function body (same variable names, same check order, same new markers `v_current_identity_names`/`COORDINATOR_HAS_LOGIN_ACCOUNT` present) - confirmed twice, once immediately after applying and once again at the end of this pass's quality gate.

**Full test matrix, direct-RPC** (real reauth proofs via the real `election_day_reauth` RPC, disposable coordinators/voters/PermissionUsers, all cleaned up after, `count = 0` confirmed each time) - **every scenario passed**:

- ADD: unique name succeeds; duplicate active `display_name` blocked; ended `display_name` reuse blocked (new); collision with another coordinator's `linked_assignment_name` blocked.
- EDIT: fully clean coordinator renames successfully; participation blocks; a voter matching `display_name` blocks; a voter matching `linked_assignment_name` blocks (new); a PermissionUser matching `display_name` blocks (new); a PermissionUser matching `linked_assignment_name` blocks (new); new-name collision with another active OR ended coordinator's `display_name` blocked; new-name collision with another coordinator's `linked_assignment_name` blocked.
- REMOVE: genuinely unused coordinator deletes; participation blocks (`COORDINATOR_LOCKED`); voter under `display_name` blocks (`COORDINATOR_HAS_ASSIGNED_VOTERS`); voter under `linked_assignment_name` blocks (new, same code); PermissionUser under `display_name` blocks (`COORDINATOR_HAS_LOGIN_ACCOUNT`, new); PermissionUser under `linked_assignment_name` blocks (new, same code) - all direct RPC calls, bypassing the UI entirely.
- **A real bug was found and fixed in the test script itself, not the RPC**: the first pass used invalid UUID literals (`'a0000000-...-0000000000p1'` - `p` is not a hex digit) for the two PermissionUser-blocking test setups, which silently failed to insert, meaning those two EDIT and two REMOVE scenarios weren't actually exercised against a real PermissionUser the first time (their renames/removes appeared to "pass" only because nothing was there to block them). Caught, fixed with valid UUIDs, and rerun cleanly - all four scenarios now genuinely proven, each confirmed via a direct post-attempt state check (the coordinator's `display_name`/existence unchanged), not just by scanning for an error string in interleaved terminal output.
- **Regression** (same session): `add`, `link`, `unlink`, a real `apply_initial_allocation_v2`, a real `rebalance_assignments_v2`, and a real `end_coordinator_activity_v2` (transfer mode) all completed correctly.

**Concurrency, re-proven against the amended definition**: two separate timed races (not reused from the prior pass) - session 1 held the shared advisory lock for a wall-clock 4-second `pg_sleep`; a concurrent `remove` call blocked for exactly 4 seconds before succeeding; a second, independent race did the same for a concurrent `edit` (rename) call, also blocking for exactly 4 seconds before succeeding. Both confirm assignment/rebalance fully serializes against both remove and rename through the existing single mutex - no new lock added, none needed.

**UI, extended**: `CoordinatorRosterEditor.tsx`'s per-row eligibility now also checks `countVotersWithRawCoordinatorName(contacts, c.linkedAssignmentName)` when set (mirroring `end_coordinator_activity_v2`'s own two-column ownership check) - proven via a dedicated Playwright pass (5/5 checks): a coordinator linked to a raw Excel string with a real matching voter correctly shows both buttons disabled with the inline reason, purely from the `linked_assignment_name` side (zero voters matching `display_name` directly). The PermissionUser-dependency check (both for `edit` and `remove`'s new `COORDINATOR_HAS_LOGIN_ACCOUNT` case) stays deliberately client-blind, same "frontend pre-checks are UX only" reasoning already established for the participation check - fetching the full permission-users roster into this component tree just to render a rare-edge-case hint was judged not worth the extra API surface; a blocked attempt surfaces through the existing toast mapper instead, now carrying a specific message ("קיים חשבון התחברות התלוי בשם זהות זה, יש לטפל בשיוך המשתמש תחילה").

**Quality**: `npx tsc -b` clean; `npm run build` clean; targeted `eslint` on all 3 further-touched application files - 0 errors; `scripts/smoke-coordinator-allocation.ts` updated with the new `COORDINATOR_HAS_LOGIN_ACCOUNT` fixture entry, full pass; `git diff --check` clean; migration-vs-live-RPC identity re-confirmed as the final quality-gate step. Dashboard Reminders and the shared Layout fix re-verified unaffected (manager card renders/groups/counts correctly, detail modal opens, scoped-role personal popup unchanged and no manager tiles leak through, `scrollbar-gutter: stable` still applied, zero overflow at 1440/390) with fresh disposable data.

**Status: IMPLEMENTED and verified against local Docker Supabase only, final invariant. Not applied to Production, not committed.**

## Election Day Layout - inconsistent white gap beside the sidebar - VERIFIED root cause, fixed (local, uncommitted)

A user report that the white gap next to the fixed right sidebar changes size when moving between Election Day screens was investigated structurally, not patched blindly.

**Ruled out first** (each independently, all 6 top-level routes plus the Allocation Management sub-view measured live via Playwright against local Docker at 1920px, geometry byte-identical across every one - `contentWidth: 1400`, `gapNextToSidebar: 132` in every case, zero variance): a per-route width/margin override (none exists - `ElectionDayShell.tsx`'s single `<div className="mx-auto max-w-[1400px]">` wraps every route's `<Outlet>` uniformly, including the Files page's conditionally-rendered `CoordinatorAllocationView`, which has no width wrapper of its own and simply inherits the shared one); a route-transition loading-state flash; any known service-worker/PWA mechanism (none exists anywhere in this codebase, already ruled out during the prior Production-UI-mismatch investigation this same day).

**VERIFIED root cause**: `AppShell`'s layout scrolls at the **document level** (the sidebar's `sticky top-0 h-dvh` positioning only makes sense against that scrolling context - confirmed no inner `overflow-y-auto` container exists anywhere in the shell), and `src/index.css` had no `scrollbar-gutter` reservation anywhere. On a real (non-headless) browser that reserves layout space for a visible scrollbar (the Windows Chrome default), a page tall enough to need a vertical scrollbar leaves less horizontal space for content than a shorter page at the identical viewport width - shifting the `mx-auto max-w-[1400px]` centered block, and with it the visible gap next to the sidebar, by the scrollbar's width, specifically on every navigation that crosses the "needs a scrollbar" threshold. **Caveat, stated honestly**: this session's headless Chromium renders with overlay (non-space-consuming) scrollbars, so the exact pixel shift could not be empirically reproduced in this environment even after deliberately forcing a 3000px-tall dummy element and re-measuring (result: 0px shift, confirming headless-here doesn't reserve gutter space at all, not that the mechanism is absent on a real browser). The diagnosis rests on structural code-level proof (document-level scroll + zero gutter reservation, the one remaining candidate after every other structural cause was directly measured and ruled out), not a reproduced screenshot diff.

**Fix**: one line, `scrollbar-gutter: stable;` added to the existing `html` rule in `src/index.css`'s `@layer base` block - reserves the scrollbar's gutter unconditionally, so content width (and the sidebar gap) stays constant regardless of whether any given page's content happens to overflow the viewport height. No page content touched, no per-page change, single shared-layout fix as required. Well-supported (Chromium 94+, Firefox 97+, Safari 18+); degrades to today's existing behavior on anything older, never worse.

**Verified**: `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).scrollbarGutter` confirms `"stable"` is applied live. A responsive sweep (1920/1440/430/390/375/360/320 px × dashboard/voters/files/reports routes, 28 combinations) found **zero horizontal overflow** introduced by the change. `npx tsc -b` clean; `npm run build` clean (new content-hashed CSS/JS filenames, expected); `git diff --check` clean on `src/index.css`. Below `md`, the sidebar doesn't render at all (`hidden md:flex`, replaced by the bottom nav bar) - the "gap next to sidebar" symptom structurally cannot occur on mobile widths, confirmed by re-reading `AppShell.tsx`, not just assumed.

**Not done, by explicit scope**: no other page/component touched; no redesign. This one-line CSS change is the entire diff.

## Manager Dashboard Reminders ("תזכורות לטיפול") - implemented, verified locally, uncommitted

New supervisory dashboard section, manager-only (`role.scopeType === "all"` - the exact complementary condition `OverdueReminderStack.tsx` already uses to decide who gets the _personal_ popup stack), placed beside `ביצועי אחראים`. **No DB/RPC/schema change required or made** - purely derived from already-fetched `scopedContacts` via the existing `resolveReminderLifecycleState`'s `"due"` state (the same single source of truth the personal popup stack already reads through - no second definition of "due" anywhere).

**Architecture**: `coordinatorReminderSupervision.ts`'s `buildCoordinatorReminderSupervision(contacts, now)` groups DUE-reminder contacts by `c.coordinator` (same plain-string grouping `coordinatorBreakdown` already uses), sorted oldest-due-first, both at the row level and within each row's own voter list. `reminderDisplay.ts` gained a new sibling formatter, `formatWaitingDuration` (natural Hebrew - "12 דקות" / "שעה ו־20 דקות", never the word "מאחר" per the product requirement). Both are pure functions, computed inside the new `CoordinatorReminderSupervisionCard.tsx` (own local ticking `now`, same tick interval `OverdueReminderStack` already uses via `APP_CONFIG.electionDayReminderPopupTickMs`) - deliberately NOT added to `useElectionDay.ts`'s central hook, so the tick only re-renders the dashboard card itself, not the whole app on every screen. The row set itself only actually changes when the underlying reminder data does, via the hook's existing Realtime subscription (`subscribeToElectionDayChanges`) - a resolved reminder disappearing, and counts/oldest-duration recalculating, is a byproduct of the exact same reactive plumbing every other dashboard breakdown already relies on, not new code.

**UI**: `CoordinatorReminderSupervisionCard.tsx` (tile grid, empty state) + `CoordinatorReminderDetailModal.tsx` (read-only table: voter / reminder time / "ממתינה כבר" - deliberately no "פתח בוחר" action, since a manager manages the coordinator's workload here, not an individual voter's case). **No "call coordinator" action** - `election_day_coordinators` has no phone field (confirmed by rereading its schema migration) and this row's `coordinator` is a plain free-text name, not even a linked coordinator entity - omitted, not stubbed, per the task's explicit "do not invent a field" instruction.

**Layout**: the existing `ביצועי אחראים` + `קצב הצבעה` row (`grid md:grid-cols-2`) became `grid md:grid-cols-3` **only when a manager is viewing** (`isManager` computed once in `ElectionDayDashboard.tsx`, not left to the card's own internal check alone) - every other role gets the byte-identical original 2-column layout, no empty middle cell. `order-*`/`md:order-*` classes give managers the product-required mobile stack order (reminders before performance) while rearranging to "performance right, reminders beside it, pace left" (RTL) at `md`+.

**A real bug found and fixed during verification, not just observed**: the new grid wrapper `<div>`s (added to support the `order-*` classes) lacked `min-w-0` - a classic flex/grid-item truncation gotcha where an unbreakable long string (confirmed with a disposable test coordinator name) forces its grid track wider than the viewport instead of truncating, reintroducing exactly the kind of horizontal overflow the same-day Layout fix (see above) had just closed. Fixed by adding `min-w-0` to the new wrapper divs, the grid container, and the new card's own inner tile grid/buttons (matching `CoordinatorPerformanceTable`'s own pre-existing `Card className="min-w-0"` convention) - re-verified clean afterward.

**Verified locally** (disposable test data - 2 test accounts, 5 test voters across 4 coordinator names covering due/future/no-reminder cases, real login, real browser via Playwright against local Docker, all cleaned up after): 0-due empty state renders cleanly; 1 coordinator with 2 due reminders and 1 coordinator with 1 due reminder both show correct tiles with correct counts and correct oldest-waiting text (spot-matched against real elapsed minutes); future and no-reminder test voters correctly never appear; detail modal shows exactly the selected coordinator's own voters (cross-verified another coordinator's voter does NOT leak in), no "פתח בוחר" button present; resolving one of two reminders (direct DB update, mirroring what the existing cancel/close RPCs do) correctly dropped the count from 2→1 and recalculated the oldest-waiting text to the remaining reminder on a fresh page load; resolving the last reminder correctly returned to the clean empty state; the scoped test account's own personal popup stack fired correctly and unchanged (2 due reminders, "יש לך 2 תזכורות לטיפול"), and the new manager-only section was correctly and completely absent for that same scoped account (0 matches) - managers do not receive the personal popup (unchanged, pre-existing, re-confirmed) and non-managers do not receive the new supervisory section (new, confirmed). Desktop card ordering confirmed via actual `getBoundingClientRect` positions (Performance right → Reminders middle → Pace left, RTL-correct). A 7-width responsive sweep (1920/1440/430/390/375/360/320) with real dashboard-reminders content loaded found **zero horizontal overflow** after the `min-w-0` fix (found 5 of 7 widths overflowing before it - see the bug above). Zero console errors throughout.

**Quality**: `npx tsc -b` clean; `npm run build` clean; targeted `eslint` on all 7 touched/new files - 0 errors (same pre-existing repo-wide CRLF-warning class only); `git diff --check` clean; `scripts/smoke-reminder-display.ts` and `scripts/smoke-follow-up-status.ts` (both protected, run read-only via their documented esbuild+node invocation, never edited) both full-pass, confirming the new `formatWaitingDuration` export didn't disturb `formatReminderDisplay`/`resolveFollowUpStatus`; `scripts/smoke-reminder-lifecycle.ts` reproduces its already-documented pre-existing, unrelated `testNonVotingReasonClosure()` failure identically (same crash, same cause) - not a new regression. Scope confirmed: 3 new files, 5 modified application files, all within the Election Day feature area; all 16 protected scripts unchanged (only executed via disposable compiled `.cjs` artifacts, immediately deleted after, source files never touched).

**Files**: `src/features/election-day/coordinatorReminderSupervision.ts` (new), `CoordinatorReminderSupervisionCard.tsx` (new), `CoordinatorReminderDetailModal.tsx` (new), `reminderDisplay.ts` (+`formatWaitingDuration`), `ElectionDayDashboard.tsx` (new prop + restructured grid row), `ElectionDayDashboardPage.tsx` (+1 prop pass-through), `election-day.constants.ts` (+`dashboard.reminderSupervision` text block). Uncommitted, alongside the still-uncommitted post-rollout documentation and the Layout fix above.

**Reverified 2026-08-21 (no code changed - the code above is unmodified from the session that built it)**: a fresh Playwright pass against local Docker with new disposable test data confirmed the count/oldest-duration/resolve/auto-update/scoped-role-isolation behavior still holds. Two of the reverification script's own checks initially reported false failures - both root-caused and corrected, not app bugs: (1) resolving a reminder via raw SQL only set `reminder_closed_at`/`reminder_closed_reason`, leaving `reminder_at` populated - `resolveReminderLifecycleState` checks `reminderAt` first and a non-null value always wins over a stale `reminderClosedReason` (by design, documented in its own header comment), so the row kept reading as "due"; the real closing RPCs always null `reminder_at` in the same statement (confirmed by rereading `20260810120000`/`20260812090200`), so the script was not mirroring real close behavior - fixed by adding `reminder_at = null` to the test SQL. (2) a whole-page `bodyText.includes(coordinatorName)` check produced a false positive/negative because `CoordinatorPerformanceTable` (a different card on the same dashboard) also lists every coordinator by name regardless of reminder state - fixed by scoping the assertion to the reminder card's own DOM subtree specifically. After both fixes, a precise re-run confirmed the exact expected sequence: 2 due → resolve one → 1 due, correct voter, oldest-waiting recalculated to the remaining voter's real elapsed time → resolve the last → clean empty state; the scoped coordinator role saw zero manager-only tiles (`button.bg-amber-50` count = 0) and did receive its own unchanged personal popup (`פתח בוחר` button present); a 7-width × 5-route responsive sweep found zero horizontal overflow. All disposable test data (accounts + voters) confirmed deleted (count = 0) after.

## Follow-up (2026-08-26) - Session endpoint deployed to Production, found non-functional (`SERVER_CONFIG_MISSING`); request-order defect fixed locally

The 4-commit Phase 3A chain (through `84a1e78`) was pushed to `origin/master` and Vercel auto-deployed it to Production (`dpl_3LvXPM2SLS3uQ74TgJYAtVE1gUSp`, READY, aliased to `kolbox-gamma.vercel.app`, build output newly includes `api/election-day/session` as a lambda). Non-mutating Production smoke testing found the Session endpoint returns `500 {"error":"SERVER_CONFIG_MISSING"}` on every method (GET/DELETE/POST all fail identically) even though both `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` and `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` are confirmed present in Vercel Production. Root cause of the 500 itself is **still unknown** - Vercel's own docs confirm a Production env var applies to "your next Production Deployment," and this deployment was created well after the var was added, so ordinary propagation timing doesn't explain it. Zero DB mutation occurred during the failed smoke test (`getServiceClient()` throws before any Supabase client is ever constructed) - Production data confirmed unchanged (1420 voters / 8 permission_users / 5 coordinators / 5 roles / 1 workspace / 0 sessions / 0 login_attempts / 0 null-workspace users, DB still 57/57).

**A real, separate defect was found and fixed locally during this investigation**: `session.ts`'s handler constructed the Supabase service client unconditionally at the top of every request, before method/Origin/cookie checks ran - so a request that should have been rejected for a cheaper reason (unsupported method, forged Origin, no cookie, blank credentials) instead surfaced `SERVER_CONFIG_MISSING` if the server secret happened to be broken, and more generally spent a DB-client construction on requests that never needed one. Fixed by moving `getServiceClient()` behind a new `requireServiceClient()` helper called only from the exact branches that genuinely need a DB round trip: POST (after Origin + body + blank-input checks), GET (after cookie-presence check), DELETE (after Origin check, and only if a cookie is actually present - a cookie-less logout stays a pure no-op needing no client at all), plus an explicit early `405` for any other method before any of that. No authentication semantics, rate-limit thresholds, cookie flags, RPC contracts, or workspace logic changed.

Verified locally against the actual bundled handler (esbuild, no framework mocking): 7/7 failure-order cases pass (no-cookie GET, forged-Origin DELETE, unsupported method, blank-credential POST, forged-Origin POST, cookie-less DELETE - all reject correctly WITHOUT `SERVER_CONFIG_MISSING` even with both env vars deliberately unset; a synthetic-cookie GET correctly still produces `SERVER_CONFIG_MISSING`, since DB resolution is genuinely required there - documented as the one intended exception). Full existing flow re-verified against local Docker Supabase with a disposable test account (workspace/role/permission-user, deleted after): login → Set-Cookie with correct flags → GET resolves identity → wrong-password rejected → rate limit still trips within the existing 10-attempt/15-min username threshold → logout clears the cookie and invalidates the session → a post-logout GET correctly returns 401. No regression found.

**A temporary, safe env-presence diagnostic was prepared locally (NOT deployed), then refined** to remove the original feature-flag design entirely: rather than a `SESSION_DIAG_LOG=1` toggle requiring a set-redeploy-unset-redeploy round trip, it now fires unconditionally on the one line that would otherwise just `throw new Error("SERVER_CONFIG_MISSING")` - so it self-gates on the real failure and needs no flag lifecycle at all. It emits one `console.log` line - visible only via the existing authenticated `vercel logs` CLI, never in any HTTP response body - containing exactly `{supabaseUrlPresent: boolean, supabaseSecretPresent: boolean, vercelEnv: string}`, and produces zero output on any success path or any request that never reaches `getServiceClient()`. Deliberately a log line rather than a new public diagnostic endpoint (even one returning only booleans would still be a new externally-reachable surface; a log line has none). Verified locally: the four failure-order cases (no-cookie GET, forged-Origin DELETE, unsupported method, cookie-less DELETE) emit no diagnostic line at all; the one genuine `SERVER_CONFIG_MISSING` case emits exactly one line with correct booleans; a success-path call with both vars present emits none. The refinement shrank the local `session.ts` diff from +71/-7 to +44/-7 by dropping the flag-lifecycle instructions and tightening comments, with no behavior change to the already-verified request-order fix.

## Follow-up (2026-08-27) - Phase 3B planning: frontend cutover architecture + pre-implementation security closure (read-only, no code changed)

Two read-only planning turns preceded Phase 3B Step 1's implementation. First, a full frontend/server architecture review: mapped every current Election Day frontend auth code path (login, localStorage session, `ElectionDayGuard`'s bootstrap detection, permission/role resolution, permission-user creation, the existing reauth-proof gate) against the already-deployed Phase 3A server contract (Migration 55 EXPAND, Migration 56 session/reauth/create-user-v3 RPCs, Migration 57's ACL hotfix). Second, a corrective pass after the first plan's Step 5 ("assign each of the 8 gated mutations its own action string") was found to overstate what was actually achievable: a full grep across `useElectionDay.ts`, `useCoordinatorAllocation.ts`, and `useRoleManagement.ts` found **12** reauth-gated mutations, not 8 (the "8" in `useElectionDayReauth.ts`'s own comment predates the role-management RPCs being wired through the same gate) - and only one of them, `create_permission_user`, has a matching `_v3` RPC that actually enforces action-binding via `election_day_verify_reauth_proof_v3`. Reading `election_day_verify_reauth_proof` (the **legacy** verifier, still used by all 11 other `_v2` RPCs) confirmed it matches purely on `proof_hash` + `expires_at`, ignoring the `action` column entirely - so a v3-issued proof for any action without a real v3 consumer would be exactly as reusable against an unrelated legacy mutation as today's proof is, bounded only by that RPC's own independent permission check, never by the proof's claimed action. This narrowed Phase 3B's real scope to session cookie cutover (login/bootstrap/logout) plus reauth+create-user for `create_permission_user` only - delete-user, reset-password, import, coordinator-allocation (4), and role-management (4) all remain on the legacy reauth mechanism as explicit Phase 3C debt, not silently implied as fixed.

## Follow-up (2026-08-27) - Phase 3B Step 1 implemented and verified locally: `POST/DELETE/GET /api/election-day/session` (`id` field added), `POST /api/election-day/reauth`, `POST /api/election-day/permission-users`

**Files changed**: `api/election-day/session.ts` (modified - added `id: row.actor_id` to both the POST-login and GET-resolve success JSON bodies; no other line touched); `api/election-day/reauth.ts` (new); `api/election-day/permission-users.ts` (new, create-only). No migration/schema file touched, no Vercel env change, no Production mutation of any kind.

**`session.ts` response change**: `id` (the actor's own bare UUID, not a secret) is now returned alongside the existing `name`/`roleId`/`workspaceId` on both POST and GET success. This is a compatibility bridge only - it lets the still-legacy `election_day_reauth(actor_id, password)` path (used by the 11 mutations that have no `_v3` RPC yet) source `actor_id` from a server-verified response instead of raw localStorage. It does **not** make `election_day_reauth`/`election_day_verify_reauth_proof`/the 11 legacy `_v2` RPCs server-trusted in any way - they remain `anon`/`authenticated`-reachable directly, bypassable by anyone holding the anon key regardless of what this endpoint returns, exactly as already documented in this file's Known Security Limitations.

**`reauth.ts`**: `POST` only, mirrors `session.ts`'s request-order hardening exactly (405 before any check → Origin → body → action allowlist → cookie presence → hash+client construction → `election_day_resolve_session` → rate limit → `election_day_reauth_v3`). Server-side action allowlist for this phase is exactly `{"create_permission_user"}`, rejected with `400 INVALID_ACTION` before any DB work for anything else - deliberately narrow, since allowlisting an action with no real `_v3` consumer would advertise a narrowing property (see the planning note above) that doesn't actually hold yet. Rate limiting reuses the existing `election_day_register_login_attempt` RPC (no new migration) under a new `reauth:actor:<verified_actor_id>` (10/15min) / `reauth:ip:<ip>` (100/15min) bucket namespace, registered only **after** a session resolves successfully - never for a forged Origin, malformed body, unsupported action, missing cookie, or unresolved session.

**`permission-users.ts`**: `POST`/create only (delete/reset-password remain Phase 3C debt, not implemented here). Accepts only `name`/`password`/`roleId`/`reauthProof` from the browser; hashes both the session cookie and the reauth proof in Node; calls `election_day_create_permission_user_v3`, which derives actor/workspace server-side from the verified session+proof and writes the caller's own `workspace_id` onto the new row. RPC error messages (`UNAUTHORIZED`/`FORBIDDEN`/`ROLE_NOT_FOUND`/the two validation messages) are mapped to fixed HTTP codes; anything unrecognized falls back to a generic `500 SERVER_ERROR` - no raw Postgres message is ever forwarded.

**Local verification** (`tsc -b` clean, `eslint --max-warnings=0` clean on all three files, `npm run build` clean and confirms `api/*.ts` is not part of the Vite/tsc build output - Vercel builds these functions separately, unaffected by this change, `git diff --check` clean):

- *Rejection paths* (esbuild-bundled handlers, no DB, `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` deliberately unset to prove these never reach the service client): 12/12 checks pass across both new endpoints (unsupported method → 405, forged Origin → 403, malformed/blank body → 400 `INVALID_REQUEST`, unsupported action → 400 `INVALID_ACTION`, missing cookie → 401 `UNAUTHORIZED` and never `500`, smuggled `actorId`/`workspaceId` body fields silently ignored). `session.ts`'s own existing 5-check request-order regression suite still passes unchanged after the `id` addition; a separate targeted test confirmed the new `id` field is present and correct on both POST/GET success while every other field, the `Set-Cookie` header, and the raw-token-never-in-body guarantee are all unaffected.
- *Positive path and security properties*, against a real local Postgres (Docker, migrations 55/56/57 applied, started with `--exclude` for the storage/studio containers that failed health checks on this machine's limited resources - a pre-existing, previously-documented local constraint, not a code defect) using disposable `__PHASE3B_LOCAL_TEST__`-tagged fixtures (two workspaces, three roles, two permission users) and a direct `pg`-connection stand-in for supabase-js's transport only (the RPC bodies executed are the real, unmodified Migration 56/57 functions - `security definer` means they run identically regardless of caller role): **24/24 checks pass**, including real login → real session cookie → real reauth proof → real user creation with correct `workspace_id`; wrong password rejected generically; a bogus `workspaceId` body field silently ignored (created user gets the caller's real workspace); cross-workspace role → `ROLE_NOT_FOUND`; an actor without `electionDay.manageUsers` → `FORBIDDEN` (reauth itself succeeds regardless of permission, matching the legacy proof's own "identity/recency only" design); garbage and expired proofs → `UNAUTHORIZED`; and - minted directly at the RPC layer to bypass this phase's own allowlist - a proof issued for a *different* action is rejected by `election_day_create_permission_user_v3`, empirically confirming the action-binding property holds for the one pair of functions that actually enforce it. Session and proof rows were confirmed to store only their sha256 hash, never the raw value. A separate 3-check suite confirmed the reused `election_day_register_login_attempt` RPC's IP-bucket threshold (101st call ⇒ count 101 > 100), that `reauth:actor:`/`reauth:ip:` never collide with login's own `name:`/`ip:` buckets even for an identical raw identifier, and that 25 genuinely concurrent increments on a fresh bucket produce exactly `{1..25}` with no lost updates. A final targeted check confirmed an invalid/unresolvable session registers **zero** rate-limit rows, matching the "only a valid resolved session reaches the counters" requirement.
- All disposable local fixtures and rate-limit rows were deleted after verification; the local Supabase stack was stopped. Production was re-verified read-only afterward: 57/57 migrations, zero drift, baseline completely unchanged (sessions=0, permission_users=8, voters=1420, coordinators=5, roles=5, workspaces=1) - this entire verification pass never touched the linked Production project at any point (the `pg` connection is hardcoded to `127.0.0.1:54322`).

**Current state**: `PHASE 3B STEP 1 LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION: PASS`. Not committed, not pushed, not deployed, no Vercel env change. Frontend cutover has still not started. The 11 reauth-gated mutations without a `_v3` RPC (`import_voters`, `delete_permission_user`, `reset_permission_user_password`, 4 coordinator-allocation actions, 4 role-management actions) remain on the legacy `election_day_reauth`/`election_day_verify_reauth_proof` mechanism - returning the session `id` field improves the normal UI path's provenance for that legacy call but does **not** make those legacy RPCs server-trusted; they stay `anon`/`authenticated`-reachable directly, same limitation as already documented. This is real, acknowledged debt for a separately-authorized future Phase 3C, not something this step resolves. Separately, the rate-limit HTTP boundary was re-proven through the **actual `/reauth` handler** (not a direct-RPC bypass): actor attempts 1-10 each returned `200` with a distinct real proof, attempt 11 returned `429 RATE_LIMITED`; 11 distinct actors sharing one IP made 100 calls that all returned `200`, the 101st returned `429 RATE_LIMITED` (15/15 checks). **What remains genuinely unverified**: a real HTTP request reaching a real Vercel Function that successfully calls real PostgREST with a real service-role key and gets a real success response has never been exercised for these two new files - this can only be closed by an actual Preview/Production deployment, not by any further local test. Direct-Postgres proof and real-Vercel-runtime rejection-path proof are both real evidence; neither is a substitute for that deployment-only confirmation, and this entry does not claim otherwise.
- **Fix 1 - fail-closed unknown-field rejection**: both `/api/election-day/reauth` (allowlist: `password`, `action`) and `/api/election-day/permission-users` (allowlist: `name`, `password`, `roleId`, `reauthProof`) now reject any request body containing a key outside its allowlist with `400 {"error":"INVALID_REQUEST"}`, checked before cookie/service-client/DB work. Previously these fields (e.g. a client-sent `actorId`/`workspaceId`/`sessionToken`) were silently ignored - never read anywhere in either handler, so never actually exploitable, but not matching this project's fail-closed preference for security-shaped fields.
- **Fix 2 - `roleId` UUID validation**: `/api/election-day/permission-users` now validates `roleId` against a syntactic UUID pattern before any DB work, returning `400 INVALID_REQUEST` for a malformed value instead of letting it reach Postgres and surface as a generic `500` via a cast failure. The DB's own authoritative same-workspace role check (`election_day_create_permission_user_v3`'s `ROLE_NOT_FOUND`) is untouched - a syntactically valid but cross-workspace or nonexistent UUID still reaches the RPC and is still rejected there, confirmed by a live regression test (below).
- **Local verification**: `tsc -b` clean, `eslint --max-warnings=0` clean (one trivial Prettier formatting fix applied), `git diff --check` clean, no `console.*`/secret material in either file. No-DB tests: 20/20 pass (exact allowed body proceeds normally for both endpoints; every named unknown field - `actorId`/`workspaceId`/`permissionUserId`/`sessionToken`/arbitrary - individually and combined -> `400`; unknown-field rejection holds even with a valid-shaped cookie present, i.e. strictly before DB work; 5 malformed `roleId` variants including a SQL-injection-shaped string -> `400`, never `500`; a valid-format UUID in uppercase still proceeds normally, proving the regex isn't over-strict). DB-touching regression (real local Postgres, disposable `__PHASE3B_HARDENING_TEST__` fixtures): 9/9 pass - a valid same-workspace UUID still creates a user (`201`, exactly one new row); a valid cross-workspace UUID still returns `ROLE_NOT_FOUND`; a malformed `roleId` and an unknown-field request both produce zero new `election_day_permission_users` rows (the RPC is never invoked). All fixtures deleted, local stack stopped, Production re-verified read-only and unchanged throughout (57/57 migrations, zero drift, same baseline: sessions=0, permission_users=8, voters=1420).
- One pre-existing local test script (`run-permission-users-rejection.cjs`'s "smuggled fields ignored" case) now correctly fails, since it asserted the OLD silently-ignored behavior this fix intentionally replaced - not a regression, superseded by the new fail-closed test suite.

## Follow-up (2026-08-27) - Phase 3B Step 2/3 frontend Session Client + Login/Bootstrap cutover implemented locally (not committed)

Three read-only architecture-closure turns preceded implementation (frontend surface mapping, `app.accessFullNavigation` trace revealing it has zero live consumer despite CLAUDE.md's stale description of it, bootstrap-scope/Zustand-sync/localStorage-cleanup decisions, then a final edge-case closure round covering bootstrap user-clearing semantics, the empty-roster-plus-session-error interaction, and login-route legacy cleanup). Implementation then proceeded under those finalized decisions.

**Files changed** (exactly the planned scope, verified via `git status`/`git diff --stat`):
- **NEW** `src/features/election-day/electionDaySessionClient.ts` - pure `fetch` wrapper (no React/Zustand dependency) around `/api/election-day/session`. `getSession()`/`login(name,password)` both resolve to a 4-way `{status: "authenticated"|"unauthenticated"|"rate_limited"|"error"}` union (`authenticated` also carries `{id,name,roleId,workspaceId}`, validated field-by-field before being trusted - a 200 response with any missing/wrong-typed field maps to `"error"`, never a partially-trusted object). Mapping: `200`+valid body -> `authenticated`; `401` -> `unauthenticated`; `429` -> `rate_limited`; `403`/`5xx`/unparseable JSON/network throw -> `error`. No internal HTTP status or server error code ever reaches a caller.
- `src/features/election-day/electionDaySession.ts` - rewritten. `user` now starts `null` (no synchronous localStorage read at module init). Adds `bootstrap()`: calls `clearLegacySession()` then `getSession()`; `authenticated` -> `set({user})`; `unauthenticated` -> `set({user:null})` (explicit clear, so a session revoked between two bootstraps in the same tab correctly logs the UI out); `error` -> no `set()` call at all, `user` is left exactly as it was - a transport/server failure is never treated as proof of logout. `login()` now calls the new client instead of the legacy `api.verifyPermissionUserLogin`, but **deliberately does not hydrate `user` on success** - the POST only proves credentials and establishes the cookie; `ElectionDayGuard`'s own GET is the sole revalidation gate before `user` is ever set. `logout()` keeps its existing reauth-proof-revoke + `set({user:null})` behavior, minus the now-obsolete `removeKey` call (nothing writes the legacy key anymore, so there's nothing to remove on logout) - it still does **not** call `DELETE /api/election-day/session` (logout cutover remains separate, not-yet-authorized work). New exported `clearLegacySession()` helper (idempotent, wraps a single `removeKey` call) - called from `bootstrap()` and from `ElectionDayLoginScreen`'s mount effect, never at module-import time.
- `src/features/election-day/ElectionDayGuard.tsx` - rewritten. Adds a second `useAsyncData(() => bootstrap())` call alongside the existing roster fetch, following the exact same established hook convention (no new `useEffect`, no manual store-sync effect - the store mutates itself inside `bootstrap()`'s own plain async body, never during React render). Waits for both fetches to resolve at least once before any routing decision (no partial-information flicker). Branch order: roster-empty is checked **before** the session-error gate (an empty roster is itself proof no legitimate session can exist, so a transient session-GET failure must never block the one legitimate onboarding use case) - `isBootstrap` reads the shared `user` field directly (`user === null`), not `sessionResult.status`, so it stays `true` on a genuinely fresh tab even while the session call errored. Every other route-access decision (error screen vs. redirect-to-login vs. authenticated Outlet) reads `sessionResult.status` - this call's own fresh result - never the shared `user` field, so a stale in-memory `user` structurally cannot grant route access while bootstrap is unresolved or errored. A session error renders a distinct connection-error screen (reusing `COMMON_TEXT.networkError`) with a retry button (`COMMON_TEXT.retry`, wired to `useAsyncData`'s `reload`) - never a silent redirect to login.
- `src/features/election-day/ElectionDayLoginScreen.tsx` - adds one mount-time `useEffect(() => clearLegacySession(), [])`, since this route is standalone (not nested under the Guard) and a user who visits it directly and abandons before submitting would otherwise never hit the Guard's own cleanup. No other change - error-copy selection (`rateLimited` vs. `invalidCredentials` vs. generic network error) is fully absorbed inside the store's `login()` action, so this screen's existing `const err = await login(...); if (err) setError(err)` contract is untouched.
- `src/features/election-day/election-day.constants.ts` - adds `session.errors.rateLimited` (no legacy equivalent existed - the pre-cutover login had no rate limiting at all).
- `src/constants/common-text.ts` - adds `retry: "נסה שוב"` (checked first - no existing generic retry label).

**Confirmed unchanged, per the finalized compatibility analysis**: `usePermissions.ts` (reads `sessionUser` reactively from the same store shape regardless of population source - zero changes needed), `AppLayout.tsx` (traced this session: `app.accessFullNavigation` has no live consumer anywhere in `src/`, contradicting its own CLAUDE.md description, which predates the 2026-08-07 Shell rebuild - `AppLayout`'s own nav items are gated by the Supabase auth role, not Election Day permissions at all), `router.tsx`, `ElectionDayShell.tsx`, `useElectionDayReauth.ts`, `electionDayReauthProof.ts` - the 11 legacy reauth-gated mutations without a `_v3` RPC still source `actorId` from `sessionUser.id`, which keeps the same shape/field name through this rewrite.

**Local verification**:

Protected script SHA-256 re-verified unchanged before and after implementation.
