## Follow-up (2026-08-27) - Phase 3B logout cutover implemented locally (not committed) - Login + Bootstrap + Logout now form one coherent trusted-session frontend

Completes the frontend session model: browser -> `DELETE /api/election-day/session` -> server revokes the session row and clears the HttpOnly cookie -> frontend clears the cached reauth proof -> Zustand `user = null`. Before this, `logout()` was local-only (cleared `user` client-side, never told the server) - the still-valid HttpOnly cookie would have silently re-authenticated the user on the very next Guard `bootstrap()` GET, making the pre-cutover "logout" cosmetic rather than real.

**Files changed** (extends the Step 2/3 files already listed above - no new files, no unrelated files):
- `electionDaySessionClient.ts` - adds `logout(): Promise<{status:"ok"|"error"}>`, a same-origin `DELETE` with no body. `200` -> `"ok"`; anything else (network throw, non-200) -> `"error"` - the deployed contract is idempotent and always returns `200` whether or not a cookie was present, and `401` is not part of this endpoint/method's contract, so it is deliberately not special-cased (per this turn's explicit instruction not to invent semantics the code doesn't require).
- `electionDaySession.ts` - `logout()` is now async and makes the real DELETE call. Adds a `loggingOut` boolean field to the store, checked/set at the very top of `logout()` - a concurrent second call while one is already in flight is a silent no-op, verified with a real overlapping-call test (not just a timing assumption), which is stronger than a component-level `busy` flag could guarantee on its own. **Failure semantics (explicit decision)**: on any non-`"ok"` result, `logout()` throws and touches nothing else - `user` is left completely untouched, and the cached reauth proof is neither cleared nor server-revoked. This was a deliberate choice, not an oversight: reporting a successful logout (or silently clearing local state) while the server-side session/cookie may still be live would let the UI show "logged out" while the browser remains fully authenticated - the security requirement this turn was built around. On success, the existing proof-revocation cleanup (best-effort `api.revokeReauthProof` + local `clearProof()`) now runs strictly *after* the server confirms the session is gone, not unconditionally as before - reviewed, not silently dropped: the cached reauth proof is a separate, shorter-lived bearer credential still used by the 11 legacy `_v2` reauth-gated mutations, independent of the session cookie, and must not survive a successful logout in the same tab. It is deliberately left untouched on a failed logout, so a still-authenticated screen doesn't unexpectedly need a fresh password re-prompt for an attempt that, from the user's perspective, didn't do anything.
- `ElectionDayShell.tsx` - the logout button now goes through `useAsyncAction(logoutAction)` (this codebase's standard mutation-wrapping hook), matching the convention every other button-triggered mutation already uses, rather than a bespoke try/catch. A thrown failure surfaces as the existing generic toast; nothing else in the button's wiring changed. `AppShell.tsx` (the shared sidebar/topbar chrome) was deliberately NOT touched - it stays a generic `onLogout: () => void` slot, unaware of Election Day's specific async/retry semantics, consistent with `AppLayout`'s own unrelated Supabase logout using the exact same slot unchanged.

**Local verification**: `tsc -b`/`npm run build`/`eslint --max-warnings=0` all clean on every changed file (zero new warnings). `git diff --check` clean. Exact scope confirmed via `git status`/`git diff --stat`. Protected script SHA-256 unchanged.
- Pure-logic (mocked `fetch`): session client's `logout()` - 4/4 (DELETE method used, `200`/`500`/`403`/network-throw map correctly). Store-level - 20/20 covering every objective case: authenticated session -> successful logout clears `user`; logout with no prior session is a clean no-op; a network *and* a `5xx` DELETE failure both throw and leave a previously-known `user` completely unchanged (`loggingOut` still resets to `false` afterward so a retry isn't permanently blocked); a real concurrent second `logout()` call while the first is in flight fires exactly one DELETE, not two; full lifecycle (bootstrap -> authenticated -> logout -> next bootstrap resolves `unauthenticated`, proving the cookie is really gone server-side, not just locally forgotten). A separate targeted suite (bundling the real, unstubbed `electionDayReauthProof` store alongside the session store to observe the actual shared instance) confirmed the proof-cleanup review outcome directly: a cached proof is cleared and server-revoked on successful logout, and left completely untouched - proof intact, revoke RPC never called - on a failed one. Full existing Step 2/3 regression suite re-run and still green: client 10/10, store 15/15 (one pre-existing test updated to `await` the now-async `logout()` with a mocked success response, since its old synchronous-call assumption no longer matched the real contract - not a behavior regression, a test-harness update), real-browser 11/11 plus the login-submit-failure 4/4, all against a freshly rebuilt production preview.
- Two test-harness bugs were found and fixed during this pass (both in scratch test infrastructure only, never in application code): two stub modules tracked call counts in a module-scoped closure variable, which esbuild inlines as a separate copy per bundle - fixed by moving both to a shared `globalThis` counter, the same class of harness issue (and the same fix) already documented from an earlier Phase 3B Step 1 verification pass.

**Current state**: `PHASE 3B LOGOUT CUTOVER LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION: PASS`. Login + Bootstrap + Logout now form one coherent trusted-session frontend cutover, entirely local: successful logout clears the real server session/cookie, the cached reauth proof, and `user`; a failed DELETE never falsely clears authenticated UI state and never silently drops the cached proof either. Reauth/create-user frontend cutover has still NOT started - the 11 legacy reauth-gated mutations without a `_v3` RPC are completely unaffected by this turn. Not committed, not pushed, not deployed, no Production/DB/Auth/Vercel mutation of any kind. Exact next step: local commit checkpoint for the full Login + Bootstrap + Logout cutover, which requires its own separate, explicit authorization.

## Follow-up (2026-08-27) - Immediate post-logout routing bug found and fixed: the prior "PASS" above did not cover this

A dedicated real-browser check (production build, Playwright network interception simulating a genuinely authenticated `ElectionDayGuard` state - local Supabase isn't running, so the roster RPC and `/api/election-day/session` were faked rather than left unreachable) found that **the previous logout implementation did not remove the user from the protected route immediately**: clicking Logout with an authenticated protected `Outlet` already mounted fired exactly one `DELETE` (200), correctly cleared Zustand's `user` to `null`, but the app stayed on `/election-day/dashboard` indefinitely - no navigation, no re-render into the login screen, until a manual reload or route change.

**Root cause**: `ElectionDayGuard`'s route-access decision intentionally reads its own `sessionResult` (from its own `useAsyncData(bootstrap)` call), never the shared `user` field directly - this is the exact design that stops a *stale* `user` from granting access while bootstrap is unresolved/errored (see the Step 2/3 architecture-closure follow-ups above). That correct design has a mirror-image gap: nothing tells the Guard's own `sessionResult` to invalidate when `user` changes for a completely different reason elsewhere (a logout click). The Guard was never wrong to ignore `user` for authorization; it simply had no signal at all that a logout had happened.

**Fix** (`ElectionDayShell.tsx` only - the smallest correct fix, not a Guard/store refactor): the logout handler now explicitly navigates to `/election-day/login` (`replace: true`) after `logoutAction()` resolves successfully, and does nothing further on failure - dropped the `useAsyncAction` wrapping from the previous turn in favor of a small direct `try/catch` (`useAsyncAction`'s return value can't distinguish success from failure, since the action resolves to `void` either way), preserving the exact same toast-on-error behavior via `toast.error(...)`. The Guard itself was **not** changed - it still authorizes strictly from its own fresh `sessionResult`, per this turn's explicit instruction; the fix is the Shell choosing to leave the protected route, not the Guard being taught to trust `user`.

**Verification, all against a rebuilt production preview with real Playwright network interception (not inferred from `user` becoming `null`)**:
- Before the fix: reproduced the bug directly (6-check script) - authenticated dashboard reached, logout clicked, exactly one DELETE fired, URL stayed on `/election-day/dashboard`, no login form.
- After the fix: same script, 6/6 pass - immediately after the click (no reload), the URL is `/election-day/login` and the login form is present.
- Failure case (D): a `500` DELETE response leaves the app on `/election-day/dashboard` (no navigation), shows the existing generic connection-error toast, and `user` is provably unaffected (already covered by the store-level tests from the prior follow-up). 4/4 pass.
- Double-click (E): two real, near-simultaneous clicks on the actual button (200ms artificial DELETE delay to widen the race window) still produce exactly one DELETE and end on the login route. 2/2 pass (plus button-found checks).
- Post-logout reload (C): after logout, a fresh `page.goto("/election-day")` (not an in-SPA check) resolves to the login route with the login form rendered, since the intercepted GET now reports `401` once the DELETE has landed. 4/4 pass.
- Full prior Step 2/3 + logout regression suite re-run unaffected: real-browser 11/11, login-submit-failure 4/4, pure-logic client 10/10 / store 15/15 / logout 20/20 / proof-cleanup 5/5 - none of these bundle or exercise `ElectionDayShell.tsx`, so an unaffected re-run was expected and confirmed, not merely assumed.

**Local verification**: `tsc -b`/`npm run build`/`eslint --max-warnings=0` clean on the one changed file (the sole warning present is the same pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` notice confirmed via a stash/pop diff in the prior follow-up, unrelated to this change). `git diff --check` clean. Exact scope: `ElectionDayShell.tsx` only - no new files, no other file touched. Protected script SHA-256 unchanged.

**Current state**: `PHASE 3B LOGIN + BOOTSTRAP + LOGOUT CUTOVER: LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION PASS (immediate-exit verified)`. The full local frontend session lifecycle now behaves coherently end-to-end, including the immediate-UI-exit property the prior "PASS" checkpoint had not actually proven. Reauth/create-user frontend cutover still has NOT started. Not committed, not pushed, not deployed, no Production/DB/Auth/Vercel mutation of any kind this turn either. Exact next step: local commit checkpoint for the full Login + Bootstrap + Logout cutover, which requires its own separate, explicit authorization.

**Independent review**: a separate read-only senior review of the diff (not a restatement of the implementation report) found no BLOCKER or HIGH findings. Two MEDIUM findings surfaced and remain **OPEN**, not fixed this pass:

## Follow-up (2026-08-27) - Two open MEDIUM findings fixed locally (not yet committed)

Both MEDIUM findings from the independent review above are now fixed in the local working tree only.

**1. Roster fetch failure** (`ElectionDayGuard.tsx`): the permission-user roster's own `useAsyncData` result (`error`/`loading`/`reload`) is now read, not just `data`. An errored roster fetch no longer falls through to the pre-existing "still loading" spinner branch forever, and is never conflated with a genuinely empty roster - the two are checked in strict order (still-loading-without-error -> spinner; error -> a dedicated error screen; only past both does an empty array grant the bootstrap exception). The session-fetch error screen's markup/copy was factored into a shared `ConnectionErrorScreen` component and reused for the roster error instead of duplicated, wired to its own `retryRoster` (independent of `retrySession`). No protected `Outlet` is reachable from either error branch.

**2. Login duplicate-submit reentrancy** (`electionDaySession.ts`): `login()` now carries a `loggingIn` boolean guard checked/set synchronously before any `await`, mirroring the existing `loggingOut` pattern on `logout()`. A suppressed duplicate call fires no network request (so it can't consume an extra rate-limited attempt) and resolves to `null` without side effects. The guard sits at the store/action boundary, not on DOM/button timing - verified to hold even when two `form.requestSubmit()` calls are issued in the same synchronous browser task (before React could re-render the button's own `disabled` state). POST success still does not hydrate `user`; `ElectionDayGuard`'s GET remains the sole revalidation gate, unchanged.

**Verification** (real Playwright browser runs against a fresh `npm run build` + `vite preview` production build on port 4323, network-intercepted per context - no local Supabase running):
- Roster fix, 14/14 checks: roster-success behavior unchanged (A); roster failure -> no stuck spinner, safe error UI, retry button, no login form, no protected nav (B1-B5); retry succeeds and resumes normal routing, exactly 2 roster calls (C1-C3); a genuine empty roster still opens bootstrap mode with no error screen and no stuck spinner (D1-D3); a simultaneous session error is shown independently and its own retry re-fetches only the session, not the roster (E1-E2).
- Login fix, 14/14 checks: two `form.requestSubmit()` calls issued synchronously produce exactly one POST and login still succeeds (F1-F2); a first failing attempt shows the correct error, re-enables the button, and a later legitimate retry succeeds with exactly 2 total POSTs, none suppressed (G1-G4); the full success path fires exactly one POST, then the Guard's own GET fires and actually hydrates the authenticated shell (H1-H4); wrong password (401), rate limiting (429), and network/server error (500) all still show their original, unchanged copy (I1-I3).
- Regression: the pre-existing immediate-post-logout-navigation suite (6/6) and the logout-failure/duplicate-click suite (7/7) were re-run against the same rebuilt preview and are unaffected by either fix.

**Local verification**: `tsc -b` and `npm run build` clean. `eslint` clean on both changed files. `git diff --check` clean (only the pre-existing CRLF advisory). Exact scope: `ElectionDayGuard.tsx` and `electionDaySession.ts` only - no other file touched, no new file added. Protected script SHA-256 unchanged (re-verified against the pre-work baseline).

**Current state**: `TWO-MEDIUM FOLLOW-UP: PASS (local only)`. Not committed, not pushed, not deployed, no Production/DB/Auth/Vercel mutation of any kind this turn. Phase 3C (reauth/create-user frontend cutover) still has not started. Exact next step: a local commit checkpoint for these two fixes, which requires its own separate, explicit authorization.

## Follow-up (2026-08-27) - Login duplicate-submit result contract: a real bug found and fixed (not yet committed)

A targeted re-verification of the `loggingIn` reentrancy guard above (using an artificially delayed first POST, both a real browser flow and a pure store-level call with zero DOM/React involvement) found a genuine, reproducible bug the earlier PASS had not actually ruled out: `login()`'s `string | null` result contract let a suppressed duplicate resolve to the same `null` a real success does. A pure-logic direct call to `useElectionDaySession.getState().login()` twice (bypassing the DOM entirely - the most decisive proof, since a browser/DOM-level test can't distinguish "the store guard suppressed the duplicate" from "the disabled button silently dropped the second submit before it ever reached `login()`") confirmed the duplicate call's promise resolved at ~20ms with `null`, well before the real, deliberately-delayed (300ms) first request settled - in BOTH the case where the real attempt was going to fail and the case where it was going to succeed. Since `ElectionDayLoginScreen.tsx`'s `submit()` read `null` as "navigate now," a real near-simultaneous double-submit could have navigated the user into `/election-day` before the real login attempt had actually resolved, regardless of whether it was ultimately going to succeed or fail.

**Fix**: `login()`'s return type changed from `Promise<string | null>` to an explicit 3-way discriminated union, `LoginActionResult = {status:"success"} | {status:"error", message} | {status:"ignored"}`. A suppressed duplicate now resolves to `{status:"ignored"}` - a value structurally impossible to confuse with `"success"`. `ElectionDayLoginScreen.tsx`'s `submit()` now switches on `result.status`: `"ignored"` takes no action at all (no navigation, no error, no re-enabling the form) and defers entirely to the real, already-in-flight attempt's own eventual resolution; `"error"` sets the error message and re-enables the form; `"success"` navigates. This is a result-contract fix, not a timing/DOM one, matching the underlying bug (which was in the contract, not in any disabled-button race).

**Verification**:
- Pure store-level, zero DOM (the decisive proof): two direct `login()` calls fired in the same synchronous tick against a mocked, artificially-delayed `fetch` - both the failing-real-attempt and succeeding-real-attempt cases confirm the duplicate resolves to `{status:"ignored"}` at ~20ms (not `"success"`), and the real call's own result (`"error"` with the correct message, or `"success"`) is unaffected. 6/6 and 6/6.
- Real browser, rebuilt production preview, artificially delayed (900ms) first POST: sampled at ~220-250ms (well before the delay elapses) across 4 repeated runs - the app stays on the login route every time in both cases; after the delay elapses, the failing case never navigates and shows the real failure message with the form re-enabled, the succeeding case navigates exactly once and only after the real POST resolved, followed by the Guard's own GET. 9/9 per run, all 4 runs.
- Regression, same rebuilt preview: the prior reentrancy suite (F-I, exactly-one-POST/later-retry/success-path/401/429/500) 14/14; the roster-error/retry suite (A-E) 14/14; the immediate-post-logout-navigation suite 6/6 - unaffected, since `logout()`'s own guard/contract was untouched by this fix.

**Local verification**: `tsc -b` and `npm run build` clean. `eslint` clean on all three changed files. `git diff --check` clean (only the pre-existing CRLF advisory). Exact scope: `electionDaySession.ts` (the `LoginActionResult` type + `login()`'s implementation) and `ElectionDayLoginScreen.tsx` (`submit()`'s handling of the new result shape) - no other file touched, no new file added, `ElectionDayGuard.tsx`/roster fix from the prior follow-up untouched. Protected script SHA-256 unchanged.

## Follow-up (2026-08-27) - Phase 3C security check: v3/legacy reauth proof cross-match vulnerability found, isolated, and locally verified (not committed)

**Before any Phase 3C frontend cutover work began**, a read-only SQL-level security check (not comment-inference) found that a v3 action-bound reauth proof (issued by `election_day_reauth_v3` for the `create_permission_user` action - the only action currently allowlisted server-side in `api/election-day/reauth.ts`) was also accepted by the LEGACY `election_day_verify_reauth_proof` helper, because both proof generations share one `election_day_reauth_proofs` table and the legacy verifier's predicate (`proof_hash = ... and expires_at > now()`) never referenced the `action` column at all. Confirmed by exact SQL, not inference: `election_day_reauth_v3`'s INSERT writes `proof_hash = sha256(rawProof)` computed in Node; Postgres's `extensions.digest(p_proof, 'sha256')` (the legacy verifier's own hashing step) produces the byte-identical digest for the same raw proof string - so a v3-issued raw proof, passed as `p_proof` into the legacy verifier, hash-matches its own row and was accepted. The reverse direction was already safe and remains unchanged: a legacy-issued proof (`action IS NULL` by construction, since `election_day_reauth`'s own INSERT never names that column) is already correctly rejected by `election_day_verify_reauth_proof_v3`'s `p.action = p_action` predicate under SQL three-valued-logic NULL comparison.

**This corrects an earlier claim from this session's own Phase 3C planning report**, which stated "the two verification queries cannot cross-match" - that was true for only one of the two directions (checked); the other direction (v3 proof accepted by the legacy verifier) was not checked before that sentence was written, and is exactly what this pass found and fixed.

**Severity: MEDIUM today** (not BLOCKER/HIGH) - every legacy `_v2` RPC that consumes the proof still independently re-checks the resolved actor's live permission before any mutation (confirmed for all 11), so this bug never let a v3 proof authorize a different actor or a permission that actor didn't already hold; and the still-fully-open, unauthenticated-at-the-RPC-boundary legacy `election_day_reauth` already hands out a strictly more powerful proof (15-minute TTL, valid against all 11 legacy actions the actor's permissions allow, zero action restriction) for the same one password check, so the bug granted no capability beyond what was already directly available. It matters forward-looking: it silently defeated the "one proof authorizes exactly one operation" guarantee Phase 3C's action-bound design exists to provide, and would have become a materially larger issue the moment a second action was added to the v3 allowlist, or once the legacy path is eventually retired. Production evidence (read-only, no mutation): the real `election_day_reauth_proofs` table holds exactly 2 rows, both `action IS NULL` (legacy-shaped), 0 with a non-null action, 0 currently valid - zero live-exploitation history, consistent with Phase 3C's frontend never yet calling the v3 reauth endpoint.

**Remediation, migrated locally**: `supabase/migrations/20260827000000_election_day_phase3c_legacy_reauth_proof_isolation.sql` - a single `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.election_day_verify_reauth_proof(p_proof text)`, identical in every respect (signature, `language plpgsql`, `security definer`, `set search_path = ''`, return shape, existing error codes/messages, ownership) except one added predicate: `and p.action is null`. No ACL/GRANT/REVOKE statement, no schema change, no TTL change, no change to v3 proof semantics (still reusable within its 5-minute window, still non-consuming - a deliberate, separate, untouched design decision), no permission/business-logic change anywhere. Comment on the function updated to document the new predicate's purpose.

**Compatibility proof** (not assumed): (1) `election_day_reauth`'s INSERT names only `(actor_id, proof_hash, expires_at)` - `action` is never set, and was added with no non-null default (`add column action text null`), so it defaults to NULL for every legacy-issued row, unconditionally, by construction; (2) no legacy consumer (`election_day_reauth`, `election_day_verify_reauth_proof`, `election_day_revoke_reauth_proof`, or any of the 11 `_v2` RPCs) references the `action` column anywhere in its body; (3) Production's real data independently confirms it empirically - both existing rows have `action IS NULL`.

**Local apply** (disposable Docker only, `supabase start --ignore-health-check`, never `--linked`): `supabase db push --db-url postgresql://postgres:postgres@127.0.0.1:54322/postgres` applied exactly and only the new migration - `upToDate:false`, `migrations:["20260827000000_..."]`, no error. Post-apply `migration list` (explicit `--db-url`, not `--linked`) confirms local 58/58, `local == remote` on every row including the new one. Production was never targeted by any apply command this turn.

**Proof-boundary regression, real local DB execution (not static inspection), 5/5 PASS**: fresh disposable workspace/role/actor fixtures + a real session row. **A** (legacy→legacy): a fresh legacy proof issued via the real `election_day_reauth` is accepted by the legacy verifier, resolved actor matches. **B** (v3→legacy, the fix under test): a fresh v3 proof issued via the real `election_day_reauth_v3` is now rejected by the legacy verifier with `UNAUTHORIZED` (pre-fix, this would have resolved the actor - PASS proves the fix works, not just that it doesn't error). **C** (v3→v3 correct action): `election_day_verify_reauth_proof_v3` accepts the v3 proof for its own `create_permission_user` action, resolved actor matches. **D** (v3→v3 wrong action): the same proof submitted with `action='delete_permission_user'` is rejected `UNAUTHORIZED` - action-binding intact. **E** (legacy→v3): the legacy proof remains rejected by the v3 verifier - unchanged, as expected.

**All 11 legacy `_v2` auth-boundary regression, real local DB execution, 11/11 PASS** (plus one bonus 12th, `create_permission_user_v2`, the currently-live frontend path, also PASS): using one real, freshly-issued legacy proof, every one of `import_voters_v2` / `delete_permission_user_v2` / `reset_permission_user_password_v2` / `manage_coordinators_v2` / `apply_initial_allocation_v2` / `rebalance_assignments_v2` / `end_coordinator_activity_v2` / `create_role_v2` / `update_role_v2` / `delete_role_v2` / `clone_role_v2` was called with minimal syntactically-valid disposable arguments and confirmed to NOT raise `UNAUTHORIZED`/`FORBIDDEN` - each either completed (e.g. `create_role_v2` genuinely created and then had its disposable role cleaned up) or raised only a benign, expected downstream business error (`USER_NOT_FOUND`/`NO_ACTIONS`/`INVALID_ASSIGNMENT_SHAPE`/`INVALID_MODE`/`ROLE_NOT_FOUND`) strictly after the auth gate - proving the added `action IS NULL` predicate does not break any of the 11 legitimate legacy flows. All disposable fixtures (workspace, role, actor, session, the one role created by the `create_role_v2` test) were deleted at the end of the same run - confirmed via a final cleanup-verification step in the same script.

**ACL before/after, verified explicitly, not assumed**: `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` does not alter privileges by Postgres design - proven empirically in this exact local database, not just cited as documented behavior: the live function body was swapped to the pre-migration definition and back (probe only, final state restored to exactly the migration file's content) with the ACL queried at all three points. Result: `{postgres=X/postgres}`, `security_definer=true`, identical across all three snapshots - **no new PUBLIC grant, no new anon grant, no new authenticated grant, no service_role change, ownership/security-definer/search_path unchanged**. (Local's ACL for this function carries no `service_role` grant at all, unlike Production's `{postgres=X/postgres,service_role=X/postgres}` - captured read-only from Production this same turn - this is the expected, already-documented local-vs-Production default-privilege divergence per CLAUDE.md's Permanent Engineering Guardrail, not a new finding, and irrelevant to this fix since no GRANT/REVOKE statement is part of this migration either way.)

**Static/scope checks**: `git diff --check` clean on the new file. `git status` shows exactly the new migration file (untracked) plus the same 15 protected scripts, unchanged - no other file modified. Secret-leak scan of the new migration file: clean (no password/key-shaped literal). Protected-script SHA-256 hashes confirmed unchanged (zero Edit/Write/Bash operation touched any of them this turn).

**Production**: not touched by any apply command this turn - only read-only queries (the 2-row proof-table count, the function-ACL snapshot) were run against the linked project, both before any local change was made. Migrations remain 57/57 on Production, this new migration is NOT applied there.

**Current state**: `LEGACY/V3 PROOF ISOLATION LOCAL: PASS`. Not committed, not pushed, no Production migration, no deploy, no frontend Phase 3C work performed. Phase 3C frontend cutover (`create_permission_user` only, per the earlier Phase 3C planning report) still has not started. Exact next step: a local commit checkpoint for this one migration file, which requires its own separate, explicit authorization; the actual Production migration apply is a further separate, later, explicit authorization after that.

## Follow-up (2026-08-27) - `create_permission_user` cut over to the trusted v3 path, implemented and verified locally (not committed)

Replaced only the `create_permission_user` legacy call chain (browser -> `api.reauth(sessionUser.id, password)` -> shared `useElectionDayReauthProof` cache -> `election_day_create_permission_user_v2`) with the already-deployed trusted v3 path (`POST /api/election-day/reauth` with `action:"create_permission_user"` -> raw proof -> immediately `POST /api/election-day/permission-users` -> `election_day_create_permission_user_v3`). The remaining 10 legacy reauth-gated actions (delete/reset-password/import/role-management/coordinator-allocation) are untouched.

**Files**: 2 new - `src/features/election-day/electionDayTrustedPermissionUserClient.ts` (pure fetch wrappers, no React/Zustand, mirrors `electionDaySessionClient.ts`'s pattern) and `src/features/election-day/useCreatePermissionUserTrusted.ts` (dedicated hook - its own `pending`/`busy` state, its own dialog instance, a synchronous in-flight guard set before any `await`). 4 modified - `useElectionDay.ts` (`addPermissionUser` now calls the new hook instead of `reauth.gate`; the now-dead `addPermissionUserRaw`/`runAddPermissionUser` removed; the legacy `election_day_create_permission_user_v2` RPC and `electionDayApi.createPermissionUser()` wrapper are left in place, unremoved, per this task's explicit "don't retire before Production-proven" instruction), `ElectionDayShell.tsx` (renders a SECOND, independent `<AllocationPasswordDialog>` instance for the new flow, never sharing state with the existing shared legacy one), `election-day.constants.ts` (new `reauth.trustedCreateErrors` copy block, including the exact required Hebrew `"שם המשתמש אינו זמין. בחר שם אחר."` for `DUPLICATE_NAME`), `api/election-day/permission-users.ts` (the duplicate-name mapping fix below).

**Proof handling**: the raw v3 proof exists only as a local `const` inside `useCreatePermissionUserTrusted.ts`'s `onConfirm` - minted by the reauth call, consumed by the immediately-following create call, discarded when the function returns. Never assigned to any `useState`/`useRef`/store/localStorage, never logged, never written to `electionDayReauthProof.ts`'s legacy cache (confirmed by code inspection: the new files import nothing from that module).

**Duplicate-username mapping (409 DUPLICATE_NAME)**: `election_day_permission_users.name` carries a real, global `unique` constraint; `election_day_create_permission_user_v3`'s INSERT has no exception handler, so a duplicate name previously fell through `mapRpcError`'s switch to a generic `500 SERVER_ERROR`. Fixed narrowly - `error.code === "23505"` AND `error.message.includes("election_day_permission_users_name_key")`, both required - deliberately narrower than this codebase's existing generic `message.includes("duplicate key")` pattern (`mapRoleRpcErrorMessage`/`mapNonVotingReasonRpcErrorMessage`) so it can never misfire on an unrelated unique-violation. **Empirically verified, not assumed**: started a local disposable Supabase instance (`supabase start`, all 58 migrations applied), seeded a disposable workspace/role/actor/session/proof via SQL, called `election_day_create_permission_user_v3` twice with the same name via `supabase-js` `.rpc()` (the real client library path) - the second call's raw error object was `{code:"23505", message:'duplicate key value violates unique constraint "election_day_permission_users_name_key"', details:"Key (name)=(...) already exists.", hint:null}`, confirming the two-factor check is reliable. Then called the REAL shipped `api/election-day/permission-users.ts` handler function directly (env vars pointed at the local instance, not Production) with a real session+proof: first call -> `201` with `workspaceId` correctly derived from the session (never client-supplied - the request body never included one), second call with the same name -> exactly `409 {error:"DUPLICATE_NAME"}`. Local disposable instance stopped (`supabase stop`) after verification; nothing in this file's test files was committed.

**Client-module mapping**: exercised the real `electionDayTrustedPermissionUserClient.ts` (via `tsx`, not a hand-copied mirror) with a mocked `fetch` covering all documented status transitions - reauth 200/401/429/network-failure and create 201/401/403/400+ROLE_NOT_FOUND/400+INVALID_REQUEST/409 - all 10 checks passed.

**Race/failure handling**: verified by code review (not a rendered runtime test - no test framework is set up in this project beyond ad-hoc Node/Playwright scripts, and a full React-render harness was judged disproportionate for this hook's size) - the in-flight guard (`inFlightRef`) is checked and set synchronously before any `await` in `onConfirm`; a failed reauth or create step toasts and returns `undefined` without resolving `pending`, so the dialog stays open and a retry always mints a fresh proof (no reuse of a failed one, no auto-retry); a `role_not_found`/`duplicate_name`/`forbidden`/`unauthorized` create result never marks `pending` resolved, so no false success is possible.

**Legacy compatibility**: `useElectionDayReauth.ts`, `electionDayReauthProof.ts`, `electionDaySession.ts`, `electionDaySessionClient.ts` are all 0-diff (not in the changed-files list) - confirmed via `git status`. The `useElectionDay.ts` diff is scoped exactly to the `addPermissionUser` block plus one import line and one return-object line; `deletePermissionUser`/`resetPermissionUserPassword`/`importFile` (still on `reauth.gate`) are byte-unchanged in the diff.

**Checks run**: `tsc -b` clean, `npm run build` succeeds (pre-existing chunk-size warning only, unrelated), `eslint` on all changed/new files - 0 errors (pre-existing CRLF-related prettier warnings across the wider codebase are unrelated to this change; the few warnings on newly-authored lines were auto-fixed), `git diff --check` clean, working-tree scope reviewed (exactly 2 new + 4 modified files, plus the same 15 protected scripts untouched), secret-leak scan of the diff clean, all 15 protected script SHA-256 hashes re-verified byte-identical to baseline both before and after this turn's work.

**Not done this turn (explicitly out of scope)**: no commit, no push, no deploy, no Production mutation of any kind, no migration/schema change, no expansion of the v3 action allowlist, no migration of the remaining 10 legacy reauth actions, no removal of `election_day_create_permission_user_v2`/`electionDayApi.createPermissionUser()`.

**Exact next step**: separate, explicit authorization to commit and push this local change, and/or to run a real end-to-end browser verification against a running dev/preview environment before any Production deploy.

## Follow-up (2026-08-28) - full local browser/runtime E2E verification of the trusted-v3 cutover: PASS, COMMIT-READY

Ran a real Playwright browser against a real local production build (`vite build` + `vite preview`, pointed at a real local disposable Supabase instance via a temporary, gitignored `.env.e2e.local` - the real `.env.local` was backed up, swapped in only for the build step, and restored immediately after the build finished, since env vars are baked into the bundle at build time). The 3 Vercel serverless routes this cutover touches (`session`/`reauth`/`permission-users`) can't be served by `vite preview` (they need the Vercel runtime, not exercised this session), so they were intercepted at the network layer - but each interceptor, for the paths where DB state matters, itself calls the SAME real local RPCs (`election_day_login_v2`/`_reauth_v3`/`_create_permission_user_v3`/`_logout_v2`) with the same session/proof-hash derivation the real handler files use, so a "success" response corresponds to a genuine row change in the local DB, not a fabricated one. This is explicitly **frontend-interception evidence for the HTTP contract layer**, composed with **real backend RPC evidence** - not a re-execution of the actual `api/election-day/*.ts` files (those were directly invoked and proven correct in the prior turn). Legacy actions (delete/reset-password) were NOT intercepted at all - they hit the real local Supabase instance exactly as the real deployed app would, since they're plain `supabase-js` RPC calls, not Vercel functions.

**1. Full success flow**: real rendered UI, real login, real navigation to `/election-day/permissions`, real form fill + role selection, real password-dialog confirm. Result: exactly 1 reauth POST, exactly 1 create POST, new user appears in the real roster (verified via the real `election_day_list_permission_users` RPC, not just the DOM), reauth body = exactly `{password, action:"create_permission_user"}`, create body = exactly `{name,password,roleId,reauthProof}` (no `actorId`/`workspaceId`), and the create response's `workspaceId` matches the session's real `workspace_id` (session-derived, never client-supplied). All PASS.

**2. Duplicate interaction/race**: widened the reauth response delay to 900ms, fired two synchronous native `click()` calls on the dialog's confirm button in the same browser JS task (stronger than two Playwright `.click()` calls, which have their own serializing actionability waits). Result: exactly 1 reauth POST, exactly 1 create POST, exactly one row created (verified via the real roster RPC) - the `inFlightRef` guard held under real DOM event dispatch, not just code review. A follow-up legitimate create attempt immediately after succeeded normally (no lock-up). All PASS.

**3. Reauth failure paths** (401/429/network-error via `route.abort()`): each produced exactly 0 create POSTs and left the dialog open for retry (no false success). All PASS.

**4. Create failure paths**: 401, 403, `ROLE_NOT_FOUND` (via a real RPC call with a nonexistent role id - genuine `ROLE_NOT_FOUND` from the DB, not synthesized), `DUPLICATE_NAME` (via a REAL unique-violation - the test attempted to create a user literally named `e2e_manager`, an existing row, producing a genuine `{code:"23505", message:'duplicate key value violates unique constraint "election_day_permission_users_name_key"'}` from Postgres, re-mapped by the SAME narrow logic as the shipped `mapRpcError`), `INVALID_REQUEST`, and network-error. Every case: exactly one fresh reauth per attempt (proving no stale-proof reuse across retries), dialog stayed open, no false success. The `DUPLICATE_NAME` case's visible toast text was asserted to be **exactly** `שם המשתמש אינו זמין. בחר שם אחר.` - confirmed verbatim. All PASS.

**5. Proof isolation**: captured the actual raw proof string sent over the wire during a real successful attempt, then asserted it does not appear anywhere in `localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, or any `console.log` output captured during the run. All PASS. (The legacy `electionDayReauthProof` zustand store's internal state was not directly inspectable via this browser harness - that specific claim rests on the code-review evidence from the prior turn, i.e. the new files import nothing from that module - stated here explicitly as a limitation, not re-claimed as newly runtime-proven.)

**6. Legacy runtime regression**: exercised real delete-permission-user and real reset-permission-user-password flows on the same page, against real fixture rows, through the real (unintercepted) legacy `election_day_reauth`/`_delete_permission_user_v2`/`_reset_permission_user_password_v2` RPCs. Both flows correctly used the shared legacy `AllocationPasswordDialog` instance (a cached proof from the delete was correctly reused with no second dialog for the immediately-following reset, exactly matching `useElectionDayReauth.ts`'s documented cached-proof behavior); the deleted row was verified actually gone via the real roster RPC; the reset password was verified to actually work via a real subsequent login call with the new password. **Decisive check**: during both legacy flows, the trusted-v3 endpoint call counters stayed at exactly 0 - proving the legacy actions never touch the new trusted create-user path. Import-voters, one coordinator-allocation flow, and one role-management flow were **not** separately runtime-exercised this turn (reaching those screens requires substantially more fixture/UI setup than was proportionate to add to an already-large verification pass) - their evidence remains the git-diff 0-change confirmation from the prior turn (`useCoordinatorAllocation.ts`/`useRoleManagement.ts`/the import code path in `useElectionDay.ts` are byte-unchanged) plus the architectural fact that they route through the exact same `useElectionDayReauth`/`reauth.gate` mechanism just runtime-proven still correct via the delete/reset flows above - stated here explicitly as a scope-trimming, not silently omitted.

**7. Session/workspace trust**: covered by items 1 and K above from real captured request bodies and the real response body - confirmed no `actorId`/`workspaceId` ever left the browser, and the created row's workspace came from the server session.

**8. Unmount/stale completion**: widened the reauth delay to 2000ms, started a create attempt, navigated away (unmounting the permissions page) ~150ms later while the request was still in flight, then waited past the delay. Zero uncaught `pageerror` events, zero console errors matching React/unmounted-component patterns. Runtime-proven, not just inspected.

**Defects found**: none. `FIX REQUIRED: NO` - no shipped code was modified this turn; only test/fixture scripts (all disposable, none committed) and this `CURRENT_STATUS.md` entry.

**Checks**: `tsc -b` clean, `git diff --check` clean, working-tree scope re-confirmed identical to the prior turn's (2 new + 5 modified files including this doc, same 15 protected scripts untouched), all 15 protected SHA-256 hashes re-verified byte-identical. `npm run build`/`eslint` were not rerun this turn since no shipped file changed (already clean from the prior turn) - reusing that evidence per this project's own "don't rerun unnecessarily" rule.

**Cleanup performed**: local disposable Supabase instance stopped (`supabase stop`), `vite preview` background server stopped, `.env.e2e.local` deleted, `dist/` build output removed, the real `.env.local` was never left swapped (restored immediately after the one build step that needed the local config).

**Current state**: `create_permission_user` trusted-v3 cutover is **COMMIT-READY** - implemented, unit/contract-tested, and now full-flow browser/runtime-verified locally across the success path, race condition, every documented failure path, proof isolation, legacy non-contamination (partial screens), and unmount safety, with zero defects found. No commit/push/deploy/Production mutation occurred. The remaining 10 legacy reauth-gated actions are unchanged. Exact next step: separate, explicit authorization to commit and push.
